Heartland Ending Explained: Charlie's Disappearance & Misty's Fate

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

This article contains discussion of sexual assault.

Netflix's soapy drama Heartland takes pages out of Nashville's playbook with a twisty plot, star-studded cast, and killer original music. And like Nashville, Heartland is divisive among fans and critics. In the vein of a more beleaguered Rayna James (Connie Britton), Jessica Chastain plays a country-legend-turned-recluse, Misty Jones, who is sucked back into Nashville when her niece goes missing.

It's not the first time that Charlie Heart (Carter Faith) hasn't shown up for rehearsals or left calls unanswered. That's just how she is. But something about this disappearance feels different, and the cops won't do anything beyond a wellness check.

Charlie's father Donny (Paul Sparks) turns to his sister for help, and Misty begrudgingly steps back into the world that spurned her 20 years ago. Heartland peels back the layers of Music Row, exposing the exploitation of young women through brutally honest storytelling. In its heightened state, Shana Feste's crime/drama, described as "watchable" by ScreenRant critic Alex Harrison, aims to burn down the house built on the backs of artists such as Loretta Lynn, Dolly Parton, and Kitty Wells. Feste is best known for directing Country Song, as well as the criminally underrated horror film Run Sweetheart Run.

Heartland explodes with two big twists. Once Misty Jones realizes the cops won't do their jobs, she exposes a family secret to get the industry's attention. She's not Charlie Heart's aunt; she's her biological mother. The bombshell comes when she pulls out Charlie's birth certificate and appears on the popular TV talk show, The Roger Lane Show. Police investigators have no choice but to take notice, but Misty's crusade comes to a screeching halt when Charlie reveals herself to be very much alive.

In a stunning revelation, Charlie felt she had no other choice but to fake her own death. Live Forever Records owner Roy Combs (Garrett Hedlund) proved to be a slimy label executive who gave Charlie her career after demanding sexual favors in exchange for undeniable success, such as touring slots and radio hits. Roy possesses photos and videos from when she first arrived in Nashville at 16 years old, and it weighs heavily on her heart. Also, Roy took out a life insurance policy on Charlie, and it was a matter of time before he would plot her death, anyway.

In that way, Heartland carries a similar tone to Nashville, the hit TV show which ran from 2012 to 2018. Nashville's Juliette Barnes (Hayden Panettiere) explored Music Row backroom dealings, love affairs, scandals, and the intricacies of contractual obligations for recording artists. Heartland takes it one step further with its fake death scenario, making the stark reality that much harder to swallow when it comes to young women in Music City. Women aren't believed unless there's a dead body. While Rayna James and Misty Jones differ wildly, they are connected by the feeling of loss—of faded dreams and desperation.

Once the dust settles, Charlie Heart, along with several other women, come forward with their allegations against Roy Combs and other industry movers and shakers. The truth forces seven men within the country music scene and the police department out into the open. Charlie finds liberation in confronting her pain, and she eventually plays the iconic venue, the Bluebird Café, in promotion of her new album. With Misty smiling in the crowd, the triumphant moment rings like an alarm bell on an industry that's been long overdue for a real reckoning. Heartland picks up where Nashville left off.

When a flabbergasted Misty Jones learns the truth, she plots with her old nemesis and fellow country legend, Winnie Jo Lean (Jennifer Nettles), to trap Roy Combs. To lure Roy, Winnie invites Misty out to sing on a high-profile stage (think: the Ryman Auditorium) and reveals that her husband's domestic violence was the cause of her busted lip back in the day. Misty took the fall, and now, Winnie hopes to make it up to her. After performing together, Roy becomes enmeshed in his own performance, giving Misty a chance to steal his laptop.

Misty takes all the photo and video evidence back to Charlie. It's up to her and how/when she decides to expose the truth. Roy eventually shows up to Winnie's mansion, where they are hiding out. Roy attempts to run Misty over in the driveway, but she shoots out his tire, flipping the truck. She's sent to prison for several months for attempted murder, but it could honestly be way worse. After suffering some near-fatal injuries, Roy Combs comes under the microscope for a new batch of sexual assault charges from Charlie Heart and numerous other women.

In the post-Me Too era, it's still remarkably refreshing when popular media continues to give women and sexual assault victims a voice. Even now, it can be difficult territory to navigate, and a streaming hit like Heartland basking in actual justice can still prove instrumental in empowering other women to come forward with their harrowing stories. It might appear that Heartland missed the boat on the popularity cemented by Nashville, but considering artists like Ella Langley dominate sales and industry accolades these days, country music remains as popular as ever. And it's never too late to share one's truth.

A former country music star steps out of seclusion to investigate her missing niece's disappearance, uncovering Nashville's dark criminal underbelly.

9 Modern Spy Thriller Shows To Watch To Prepare For Robert Pattinson’s Bond Era

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The next era of James Bond is closer than ever, and with Robert Pattinson officially cast as James Bond, there's a lot to look forward to now that Amazon MGM is behind the wheel.

With Denis Villeneuve directing and a script from Steven Knight, the creative team behind the next James Bond film is now complete, with Pattinson tapped as the future 007. Expectations and stakes are high for the coming take on the franchise. As James Bond continues to push boundaries in the espionage genre, it would be smart to look to the best spy thriller TV shows that are capturing the attention of audiences everywhere.

Tom Hiddleston, the star of The Night Manager​​​​​​, has a James Bond energy himself, and he brings this gravitas to the role of Jonathan Pine in the critically acclaimed series. Set in Cairo, The Night Manager follows Jonathan, the titular evening manager of a high-end hotel, as he becomes involved with a dangerous ring of arms dealers and the agents trying to bring them down.

Also featuring Hugh Laurie, Olivia Colman, and Noah Jupe, The Night Manager's sleek style and complex storylines are elevated by the skill of the central cast. Laurie plays a villain worthy of any James Bond movie, and the dynamic between him and Hiddleston throughout the episodes will keep audiences on the edge of their seats.

Enormously popular, sharp, and proof that Apple TV can tackle thriller TV shows as well as science fiction, Slow Horses has been setting the tone for contemporary spy series since 2022. The announcement of Slow Horses' season 9 and 10 renewal doesn't come as a surprise, as the show has found a dedicated audience base thanks to the beloved cast led by Gary Oldman and Jack Lowden.

Also based on a book series, like James Bond, Slow Horses follows a group of disgruntled and low-achieving MI5 agents sent to Slough House to do the drudgery of the agency. However, somehow, the characters find themselves caught up in thrilling investigations. Though they lack the smooth grace of James Bond characters, this makes them even more interesting to watch.

A period drama, neo-noir, and a must-watch non-English-language series for audiences who haven't yet discovered it, Babylon Berlin is set in Berlin beginning in 1929. Following the rise to power of the Nazi Party and the fallout of World War I, Babylon Berlin is a fresh take on this period of history, which has been brought to life onscreen many times before.

Volker Bruch leads the cast as Inspector Gereon Rath, a tortured police officer caught up in a deeper conspiracy than he could ever imagine when he enters the seedy underbelly of the city. Though James Bond has always appealed to international audiences, the warm critical and audience reception of Babylon Berlin makes it clear that viewers are ready to see their heroes in espionage stories complicated and full of anguish.

Zoe Saldaña has made her mark on most of the biggest movie franchises of the 21st century, from The Avengers to Avatar​​​​​​. However, her recent work in the thriller series Lioness shows Saldaña giving one of the best performances of her career. She plays Joe McNamara, the CIA agent heading up the titular Lioness program by recruiting female agents.

One of the most exciting aspects of modern thriller TV shows is how ready they are to put women in leading roles and give them juicy espionage storylines up to par with the legacy of James Bond and other classic spy narratives. As the series has progressed, Lioness has only gotten better, crafting complex storylines and giving Saldaña and her co-stars plenty of opportunities to shine in action-oriented and nuanced emotional moments.

Black Doves season 2 recently dropped on Netflix, and the series' second outing has proven to be just as exciting, cementing it as one of the streamer's best thriller shows of the decade. With Keira Knightley and Ben Whishaw leading the cast as Helen Webb and Sam Young, a pair of spies-for-hire, Black Doves is a twisting narrative of secrets, betrayals, and moral questions that the lead characters are forced to face throughout the seasons.

It's fun to watch Whishaw's performance in Black Doves, as it differs sharply from his work as Q in James Bond. In Black Doves, he gets the chance to step into the action-oriented side of spy work. Black Doves leans into the fun tropes of spy thrillers while pushing boundaries in ways that major franchises often struggle to do. Focusing on Helen as a female spy and Sam as a gay man, Black Doves expands the definition of a traditional espionage series to great effect.

Netflix's spy series Black Doves has cast Daniel Craig's Q, Ben Whishaw, as a James Bond-style cold-blooded assassin with a license to kill.

Based on the French series The Bureau, Paramount+'s The Agency boasts a star-studded cast, featuring some James Bond veterans of its own. Led by Michael Fassbender as Brandon Colby AKA "Martian," The Agency sees Brandon adjust to new assignments and politics after being pulled from undercover work. Jeffrey Wright co-stars as Henry Ogletree​​​​​​, Brandon's supervisor and confidant. Wright clearly draws upon his work in the Craig James Bond films as Felix Leiter in the series.

Though The Agency season 3 hasn't been officially confirmed for renewal, hopes are high for fans of the espionage show. Dealing with the relationship between dueling intelligence agencies and the intensity of being immersed in complex webs of secrecy, The Agency is reminiscent of later James Bond movies, which see Bond reflect on the toll his life as an agent has taken.

It's difficult to imagine anyone other than Sandra Oh and Jodie Comer playing Eve and Villanelle, the MI6 agent and brutal assassin who embark upon a twisted, intense obsession with each other in Killing Eve. The chemistry between Oh and Comer makes the intensity and depth of the love-hate relationship between Eve and Villanelle so compelling, and makes it impossible not to become obsessed with how their doomed romance will end.

Though Killing Eve received mixed reviews in the series' later seasons, it's worth sticking with the show until the end to discover what happens between Eve and Villanelle. James Bond has often faced criticism for its depiction of female characters, and while the Craig era corrected many of these dated choices, Pattinson's turn as Bond could learn from Killing Eve's complex depiction of female spies and assassins.

Eddie Redmayne is unrecognizable in The Day of the Jackal, Peacock's recent reimagining of the classic 1973 thriller film. As the Jackal, or Alexander, Redmayne is a ruthless, cold-blooded killer that the audience can't help rooting for. The Day of the Jackal chronicles the cat-and-mouse game between the Jackal, a world-renowned assassin, and the MI6 agent Bianca (Lashana Lynch), who is hot on his trail.

Peacock has now finally confirmed when Eddie Redmayne's TV assassin will be returning to the small screen for The Day of the Jackal season 2.

The Day of the Jackal has a lot in common with a traditional Bond narrative. Globe-trotting, glamorous, and full of beautifully designed weapons that make the Jackal nearly untraceable, the series doesn't skimp on tense action sequences or gorgeous set pieces. The Jackal is the kind of villain that Bond himself might come up against, and one that would make the viewer question who they want to win in a battle between the two.

Arguably the best spy thriller of the 21st century, The Americans is a dark, brilliantly-paced take on the Reagan era of the Cold War. It stars Keri Russell and Matthew Rhys as Elizabeth and Phillip Jennings, two Soviet KGB officers posing as American citizens and raising a family outside Washington, D.C. Grappling with the psychic toll of their double life and the life-and-death stakes of their work, The Americans only gets better as the seasons progress.

Though The Americans isn't as flashy or gadget-oriented as some other espionage thrillers, its realism and the gritty depictions of violence and manipulation make it stand apart from other additions to the genre. As James Bond enters a new era, the popularity of series like The Americans will influence how the franchise evolves, and any audience member who appreciates a smart spy series will fall in love with The Americans.

The Best Hostess Gifts to Bring to a Holiday Party

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Over 75 ideas to carry you throughout the season

It’s an established social etiquette rule that showing up to a party empty-handed is a bit of a faux pas. Of course, there are more intimate gatherings where a simple bottle of wine or an extra cheese for the board will suffice, but the holiday season runs on a different track. Homes are dressed to the nines, menus are calibrated to merrier flavors, and the stakes somehow feel higher. Arriving with a gift for the hostess not only signals your appreciation, but also adds to the evening’s celebratory air. But what to bring?

There’s zero shame in going the traditional route—no one is going to turn their nose up at a Le Labo candle, after all. If your host is a hardcore holiday enthusiast, why not bring a handcrafted ornament to dress their tree with? Or a fresh stack of illustrated Christmas cards? Something pretty for the pantry will always go to great use, and if you have a strong sense of their taste in interiors, a sleek vase or decorative tray will surely make your host feel extra special.

This year, rather than scrambling to secure the best hostess gifts in the 11th hour, save yourself the stress and stock up ahead of time. Below, shop over 75 picks (starting at just $12) to bring along to all your upcoming holiday fêtes.

For the serial hosts who regularly throw dinner parties, gift them a beautiful tablecloth for the season, or an artisanal set of napkin rings. If you’re hoping they might use your gift the same night of the party, Gohar’s clever crumb catcher could easily be placed on the table for pre-dessert tidying.

High-Gloss Furniture Will Be Everywhere in 2027—Here's How Designers Make It Feel Fresh

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

A single lacquered piece can make a neutral room feel instantly more interesting.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

After years of quiet luxury, soft neutrals, and interiors that favor understated details, a little drama feels like a very welcome change. High-gloss furniture is making a comeback, bringing shine, color, and a bit of personality back into our homes. Whether it's a lacquered accent table, a sculptural console, or a statement cabinet with a reflective finish, these pieces have a way of catching your eye and making even the simplest room feel more interesting. And honestly, who says every piece of furniture has to blend into the background?

The look also ties into Neo Deco, a modern take on Art Deco that brings together glamorous materials, bold silhouettes, and a fresh approach to decorating. But you don't need to turn your living room into a scene from The Great Gatsby to get in on it—a single glossy piece can add just the right amount of contrast to a neutral space, while mixing lacquer with natural wood, textured upholstery, or warm metallic accents keeps things feeling layered and livable.

Ahead, designers share how to bring high-gloss furniture into your home and embrace a little more shine without going over the top.

Not ready to go all-in on the high-gloss trend? An accent table is your low-commitment, high-impact way in. Tucked beside your favorite armchair or pulled up next to the sofa, a lacquered side table brings just enough shine to make the whole corner feel a little more interesting. The best part is the contrast, so have fun mixing finishes. Carol Alda, interior designer at Z Gallerie, loves pairing a glossy table with a “velvet or textured woven chair.” Just make sure the table sits at a comfortable height next to the chair so you can actually use it for your coffee (or cocktail).

Color is another way to make the piece feel right at home. Ali Esmaili, design expert and owner of Esmaili Rugs, suggests pulling the shade of your accent table from a patterned rug, which creates an easy connection between the two. An oxblood, emerald, or classic black table brings plenty of Neo Deco attitude, especially in a sculptural shape. It's a small swap, but one that can give even the most neutral living room a welcome dose of personality.

A little high-gloss feels right at home in a bar or entertaining area, where a touch of drama is practically part of the assignment. A lacquered bar credenza or sideboard in a saturated shade can turn your glassware and favorite bottles into a whole display moment.

Jennifer Fitzpatrick, founder of Studio J & Co., explains that high-gloss finishes bring extra energy to a room by reflecting daylight and catching the flicker of candles and lamps in the evening. In a bar area, that means your decanters, glassware, and cocktail accessories get their own little spotlight. Fitzpatrick recommends pairing high-gloss pieces with unlacquered brass, fluted glass, and textured textiles.

The coffee table is already the unofficial centerpiece of the living room, so why not give it a little extra attention? A lacquered version in a rich color or an unexpected shape can instantly make the space feel more interesting. And don't worry about all that shine feeling too formal. Fitzpatrick says today's high-gloss pieces are all about sculptural shapes, from curved profiles to clean geometric forms. A lacquered cocktail table can also catch the glow of your lamps in the evening, adding a little atmosphere without visually crowding your seating arrangement.

To keep the look feeling relaxed, mix that reflective surface with pieces that have plenty of texture. Esmaili loves pairing high-gloss furniture with vintage rugs, noting that “the gloss adds depth, movement and a touch of theatre.” The contrast between a shiny coffee table and a well-loved rug with plenty of pattern and texture gives the room a more layered, lived-in feel. Add a soft sectional and a few simple accessories, and you've got a statement piece that still feels right at home.

King Charles Had the Most Unexpected Response to Robert Pattinson Being Announced as James Bond

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The royals have long been fans of the franchise.

King Charles and Queen Camilla had the best response to the long-awaited news that Twilight and Batman legend Robert Pattinson is set to become the new James Bond.

The royal family has long been associated with the iconic British spy series, with the King and Queen—along with Prince William and Catherine, Princess of Wales—attending the No Time to Die premiere at London’s Royal Albert Hall in September 2021 (the last film that was released in the long-running franchise).

In response to the official James Bond X account sharing the news about Pattinson, the royal family's account (which acts as a news source for the King and Queen), re-shared the post along with the comment: “Welcome to His Majesty’s Secret Service, Mr Bond.”

So far, the quip has been liked over 250,000 times.

Prior to the 2021 premiere, the King had attended plenty of other screenings of the Bond series—dating as far back as 1981 for the launch of For Your Eyes Only, which had Roger Moore starring as the fictional MI6 agent.

The late Princess Diana and Princess Margaret also turned out for that particular premiere.

Commenting on his major career leap, Robert Pattinson said in a statement, “James Bond is one of the most legendary characters in cinema, and to be entrusted with 007 is the honor of a lifetime.”

He continued, “I’m deeply grateful to Denis, Amy, David, Tanya, and everyone at Amazon MGM Studios for their belief in me. To say this role is a dream come true is an understatement. I couldn’t be more excited about this partnership and the work ahead with this fantastic team.”

Filming is expected to begin early next year, with an unspecified release date slated for sometime in 2028.

It will be the first James Bond movie produced by Amazon MGM, after the Broccoli family unexpectedly sold the rights in 2025.

'SNL' Alum Heidi Gardner: No Drugs Found at Scene of Boyfriend's Death

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Heidi Gardner's boyfriend, Ray Tintori, was found dead inside his New York City apartment last week ... but a new report says no drug paraphernalia was discovered at the scene.

According to Page Six, a source says the filmmaker's death did not appear to be criminal in nature ... and there was no drug paraphernalia found inside his East Village apartment.

The outlet also reports Tintori's friends discovered his body November 10 ... meaning he wasn't found by the former "Saturday Night Live" star.

As TMZ first reported Monday ... Tintori's death remains under investigation, with the New York City Office of Chief Medical Examiner telling us his cause and manner of death are pending further review.

Sources with direct knowledge also told TMZ Heidi is grieving the loss and is surrounded by family and friends during this difficult time.

It's unclear exactly when the couple began dating ... but Tintori shared a heartfelt birthday tribute to Heidi in July 2025, calling her "Prettier and more graceful than the moon and by far the coolest person I've ever met."

Tintori was known for directing short films and music videos ... and was part of Court 13, the filmmaking collective behind the Oscar-nominated movie "Beasts of the Southern Wild."

Penn & Teller Join Short Film ‘The Inspector’ as “Executive Magic Producers” (Exclusive)

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Spanish short is written and directed by Jandro, one of Spain's biggest magician acts.

Penn & Teller have pulled off their newest trick, coming on board Spanish short film The Inspector as its “executive magic producers.”

The move puts the pair in league with Spanish magician, filmmaker and television personality Jandro, who wrote, directed, and produced the dark comedy thriller, which is seeking to become an Oscar contender this awards season. The parties already worked together when Janto appeared on pair’s magic competition series Fool Us.

Penn and Teller will now lend their support and name for the movie’s awards campaign. Related Stories TV The CW Sets Biggest-Ever Summer Lineup With 11 Series TV Alyson Hannigan Joins 'Penn & Teller: Fool Us' as Host

Inspector, set in Spain, tells the story of a fraudulent gas inspector who targets vulnerable elderly people, exploiting their trust to gain access to their homes and steal their valuables. His next victim is Carmen, a sweet grandmother with a collection of valuable antiques. He soon gets more than he bargained for. The 12-minute, genre-bending short film combines thriller, mystery and dark comedy.

“Jandro, like Hitchcock, understands cinematic sleight of hand. The Inspector is my kind of movie,” said Teller in a statement. “If you like Penn & Teller, you’ll love The Inspector. It’s tricky.”

Jandro is one of Spain’s most well-known magicians and one of the most recognizable faces in Spanish television, having spent 15 years as a writer and performer on the popular Spanish late-night show El Hormiguero. He earned fame in magic circles by hoodwinking Penn & teller as part of their Fool Us competition series. He won that contest six times. He also won the award for Spanish Champion of Comedy Magic in his home country.

Inspector has already been making its own magic on the festival circuit. The short has played in roughly 200 festivals, winning 50 awards across 22 countries. The film has played at four Oscar qualifying festivals including Sitges in Spain, ShortShorts in Japan, Ireland’s Foyle, and Edmonton in Canada. It is now vying for an Oscar nom.

Dune Magic: The Gathering Crossover Officially Confirmed After Major Leak

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Arrakis, its noble houses, and its legendary sandworms are getting the Magic card treatment

The spice must flow, and so must the Secret Lair drops. After several days of leaks circulating online, Dune is officially crossing over with Magic: The Gathering, with Wizards of the Coast teasing the collaboration through the official MTG Secret Lair account.

The announcement follows a series of leaked promotional images that surfaced on Reddit and Discord on Nov. 12, giving players an early look at what appeared to be three Dune-themed Secret Lair collections. The official teaser now confirms that the collaboration is real.

The superdrop is expected to arrive on the Secret Lair website on Nov. 30, giving fans a little time to prepare their spice reserves.

According to the leaked images, the collection features three separate drops. The first introduces eight mechanically unique legendary creatures based on characters from the franchise, including Paul Atreides, Chani, Baron Vladimir Harkonnen, Princess Irulan, and Reverend Mother Jessica.

The second collection focuses on Arrakis and its enormous sandworms, introducing Shai-Hulud as a new Magic card alongside Dune-inspired reprints. The third, titled Spice Harvest, features familiar cards reimagined as items and technology from the franchise. Among the leaked reprints are Darksteel Forge, Revel in Riches, and Halo Fountain.

The leaks have generated plenty of excitement among Magic players, with some already discussing potential Commander decks built around the new characters. Others were less enthusiastic about the use of movie actors' likenesses instead of original character artwork, while several fans were quick to ask whether Duncan Idaho would make the cut.

The timing also makes sense for the crossover. Dune: Part Three is scheduled to hit theaters on Dec. 18, 2026, just weeks after the Secret Lair collection arrives. For now, players won't have to wait much longer to see how much of Arrakis makes its way into their Magic collections.

The Best Coffee-Table Books to Gift Your Beautiful, Discerning Friends

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

These books will please everyone on your gift list this year

Every year holidays come around, and a slight panic sets in at the thought of finding gifts that the people you love will get excited about—and that will make them feel seen. The real trick is to keep a list throughout the year of everything you see that reminds you of this person or that person. But because sometimes you don’t write things down because you’re “sure you’ll remember,” and because even if you do, we need all the help we can get in discovering cool things, we’ve put together a list of our favorite coffee-table books to give (and to receive, if we’re being honest) this year.

From fashion monographs to punk rock album covers to a guide to medieval wellness, we promise that we’ve found something that will thrill even your most cynical friend.

Richard Avedon was undoubtedly one of the greatest photographers of the 20th century and this collection gathers his work for Harper’s Bazaar from 1944 to 1965. As the world experienced generation-defining shifts, Avedon was zeroed in on his style, creating indelible images that would go on to change fashion. Bonus? It pairs perfectly with Ron Howard’s documentary on the photographer, also titled simply Avedon.

This massive pink tome, assembled by Coppola herself from her own collection of photographs, scripts, collages, and all manner of feminine detritus, offers a revealing and inspirational look at the director’s process and world-building.

With wit and humor, medievalist Larisa Grollemond sheds light on the way humans lived—and thrived(?)—during the middle ages. Is it true they never bathed? Did they have practice birth control? Were witches in fact, doctors? This books holds the answers.

Raymond Pettibon may now be a certified blue-chip artist, but he began his career in the 1970s drawing flyers and record covers for punk bands in Southen California like Minutemen and Black Flag. (He also designed their iconic four-bar logo, which has now been meme-fied.) This book collects his musical body of work, which also includes the Foo Fighters and Lana del Rey—that’s his work on the cover of Norman Fucking Rockwell.

The elusive Martin Margiela may very well be the designer that has most influenced contemporary fashion. Nowhere is that more evident than in this book that looks at the time when the Belgian designer was at the helm of Hermés. The enduring appeal of the collections he created from 1997 to 2002 would go on to set the tone for the whole quiet luxury moment we’ve been living under—except better.

Jeff Goodwin was the makeup artist for David Lynch’s iconic film Blue Velvet. His personal trove of Polaroid photos capture impromptu behind-the-scenes moments and reveal how he refined the makeup looks; they’re accompanied by his own memories of the filming experience.

How many people’s fashion obsessions began as they endlessly dressed and undressed their dolls—be they Barbie, Bratz, or even American Girl dolls? This tome looks at the symbiotic relationship between dolls and real-life grown-up fashion, zeroing in on the works of designers like Comme des Garçons’ Rei Kawakubo and Viktor & Rolf’s Viktor Horsting and Rolf Snoeren, among others.

This is a book designed to inspire. For “Kitchen Piece,” Ono suggests one “hangs a canvas on a wall” and “throws all the leftovers you have” on it, with the caveat that “you may prepare special food for the piece.” After spending time with Ono’s instructions, one realizes there is art to be found literally everywhere.

This is the only book about Carolyn Bessette Kennedy that one may ever need. Sunita Kumar Nair talks to CBK’s friends and, through a carefully curated edit of images, breaks down her style. She also reveals the true essence of the person behind all those infinitely rebloggable images.

Past Continuous is the name of an exhibit at the Metropolitan Museum of Art in New York that brings together the world of Lee Krasner and her husband Jackson Pollock and looks at their career arcs in tandem. It begins with a selection of their paintings before the pair met, and traces their influences, taking a closer look at how their presences wove in and out of each other’s work.

This tome celebrates America’s 250th anniversary through over 200 pages filled with iconic images that have defined the country throughout its history, from images of cowboys wearing their worn-in Levi’s and immigrants landing at Ellis Island to Martin Luther King and Elvis Presley.

Diane Arbus’s unflinching photographs captured humanity in all its beautiful and exacting rawness. Documents tells the history of her short-lived career through essays, criticism, and other ephemera from 1967 through the present day; it’s a comprehensive guide to understanding her work.

Glen Luchford photographed a few of Prada’s most iconic ad campaigns of the 1990s. This book brings them all together along with alternate shots and test shots, and is an essential for both Prada aficionados and lovers of the decade.

Created in 1909 by Arthur E. Waite and Pamela Colman Smith in London, the appropriately titled Waite Smith tarot deck is the most popular deck in the world. This book looks at its history, uncovering the details and meaning of each individual card—and it comes with its own set of cards.

This year, the Fashion Museum Antwerp is celebrating its 40th anniversary with an exhibition dedicated to the “Antwerp Six,” the name the fashion media gave to a group of designers—Dries van Noten, Ann Demeulemeeter, Marina Yee, Dirk Bikkembergs, Walter Van Beirendonck, and Dirk Van Seene—that arrived in London in the mid ’80s fresh after their graduation from the Royal Academy of Fine Art. For those who can’t make it to Belgium to see the show, the book may prove to be the next best thing, chock full of photographs, illustrations, newspaper clippings, and essays. They may not have all become household names, but their influence can still be felt on the runways today.

Brianna Capozzi’s unique view of womanhood has made her one of the most sought-after photographers in fashion. Her latest monograph features a mix of magazine shots of famous singers and actors with images of impromptu shoots staged with her best friends. The result is a visual shot of adrenaline and a reminder that girls—well, women—just wanna have fun!

James Blunt Says He Legally Changed His Name to Ryan Air: Here's Why

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

James Blunt said he legally changed his name to Ryan Air—in reference to the budget airline Ryanair—after his 2026 album Airplane Mode claimed the No. 1 spot on the UK music charts.

James Blunt is saying goodbye to his name.

After the “You’re Beautiful” singer vowed to legally change his moniker to Ryan Air if his 2026 album Airplane Mode made it to the top spot on the UK music charts, it appears he’s making good on his promise.

In fact, the 52-year-old revealed in a screenshot shared to Instagram Nov. 16 that he changed his full name from James Hillier Blount to Ryan Bel Air following the album’s success.

And it seems the weight of it all wasn’t too heavy. After all, the “Goodbye My Lover” singer captioned the post, “This cost me £53.05.”

Nearly a week earlier, Blunt made a public promise to change his name should his album, which dropped Nov. 6, get the top spot on the music charts—a proclamation he’s made in the past.

“Hi, I’m James Blunt—and you f--ked it,” he explained in a Nov. 11 Instagram video. “Two years ago, I promised to legally change my name to Blunty McBluntface if my album made it to number one on the charts. Didn't happen. Your fault. So now, I'm giving you a second chance.”

“You may have heard that my new album Airplane Mode is out now,” he continued, “and if it makes it to number one, I will legally change my name to Ryan Air.”

Getting some practice with the moniker, Blunt, dad to two kids with wife Sofia Wellesley, went on to say repeatedly, “The name's Air—Ryan Air.”

“So, stream it, buy it, just get it to number one,” he said, “or else I'll have to carry on being James Blunt. Nobody wants that.”

And if his new name sounds familiar, that’s because it’s inspired by budget airline Ryanair. At the time, the company trolled his video, writing on X, “At least people would know you then.”

Airplane Mode marks his first UK number one album in 19 years, according to the Official Charts Company. And while the Celebrity Traitors alum is known for his funny side, he got serious when it came to talking about the accomplishment.

“I can’t tell you how grateful I am to every single person who has gone out there and downloaded, or bought, or streamed my album,” he told the Official Charts Company Nov. 13. “From the bottom of my heart, thank you so much.”

As for how he plans to commemorate the milestone, Blunt added, “I shall celebrate by getting absolutely pissed.”

Keep reading for more celebs and their real names…

Congratulations to Post Malone, who incorporated his surname into his stage name. However, the rapper does prefer his close friends to call him by his real name.

During the 2024 MTV VMAs, Taylor Swift referred to him as "Austin" when they won the Best Collaboration award for their duet "Fortnight."

Though she portrayed Lilly on Modern Family under the name Aubrey Anderson-Emmons, the actress changed her moniker to Frances Anderson to mark her era as a musician.

“Frances is actually part of my legal name—it’s my middle name, and it’s after my mom’s old family friend,” she explained in a 2025 E! News interview. “I thought, one, it was a bit shorter than 'Aubrey Anderson-Emmons.' I do have a long, hyphenated last name.”

The siblings grew up going up by their middle names, which they chose to use when they entered Hollywood: Dakota Fanning and Elle Fanning.

As Elle explained, because their mother went by her middle name, it made sense for the sisters to do the same.

“It was awkward in school when they were calling roll call,” she recalled to Glamour in 2016, “because they’d go, ‘Mary? Mary?’ Kids would be like, ‘There’s no Mary here.’ I’d awkwardly raise my hand, like, ‘Uh, it’s me.’”

The Summer House star shocked Bravo fans by revealing his legally given first name isn't actually Carl.

"My I.D. and my passport—my first name is William," Carl—who has appeared on the reality show for all nine seasons—told PopViewers in August 2025. "So, my middle name is Carl, but I've always, since day one, been called Carl."

Sterling K. Brown went by his middle name Kelby until he was a teenager.

"My dad's name is Sterling Brown Jr., my grandfather is Sterling Brown Sr.," he said during an April 2025 appearance on The Kelly Clarkson Show. "I wanted my own name and it felt like Sterling was an old man's name."

But years after his dad died from a heart attack, the This Is Us alum decided to revert back to his first name as a way to honor his father.

"Because he passed away when I was 10, by the time I turned 16 and I hadn't heard his name for five, five-and-a-half years,” he continued. “I was like, ‘I kinda just want to hear that name again.’ So I asked people to call me Sterling."

The Traitors winner shared on E!'s Hot Goss that his real name is Nicholas Dylan Harrison Efron, but he's "axed off" his first name and prefers Dylan. As he explained, "That's my first name and everything gets bumped up," making Harrison his middle name now.

Andrew Mignogna, Matthew Mignogna & Joey Mignogna

Andrew Lawrence, Matthew Lawrence and Joey Lawrence adopted a new last name in their careers. Joey—whose middle name is Lawrence—was the first to do so after a talent agent criticized the family surname.

Kat Dennings adopted a stage name at age 9, years before she made her official onstage acting debut on a 2000 episode of Sex and the City.

The 2 Broke Girls alum told Kylie Kelce on the March 13, 2025 episode of her podcast Not Gonna Lie With Kylie Kelce that she didn't think her real name name should "be displayed on a poster."

The actress drew inspiration for her stage name from Christina Ricci's character Kat in the film Casper as well as the surname of Janine Denni, the French wife of late The Black Cauldron author Lloyd Alexander, a writer she said was her mother's best friend.

For more than three decades, Cher was made to believe that her legal first name was Cherilyn. It wasn't until the late '70s, when she obtained her birth certificate in order to change her full name to just Cher, that she discovered that she had been registered as "Cheryl."

In her 2024 memoir, Cher: The Memoir, Part One, the pop icon recalled her mom Georgia Holt's reaction to this discovery.

She said her mother, who gave birth to her at age 19, responded, "Let me look at that!" and then, recalling her postpartum experience, added, "I was only a teenager, and I was in a lot of pain. Give me a break."

The outlaw country star adopted Shaboozey as his nickname and later used it as his official alias as a music artist after his Virginia high school misspelled his surname that way.

Though the singer initially released a Christian album under her birth name in 2001, she felt the stage name Katy Perry—inspired by her mom's maiden name—might leave pop fans more in awe, awe, awe.

Cardi B took on the name Bacardi after family and friends started calling her sister Hennessy. Later on she shortened the name to something that suited her quite a bit more.

Before she became the Duchess of Sussex, Meghan Markle was briefly known as Rachel.

Shania Twain shared the origin story of her stage name during an appearance on Apple Music's Home Now Radio: "In short, I was born, Eilleen Regina Edwards, and then I was adopted and I became Eilleen Regina Twain. Then I became a professional singer and I needed a stage name that sounded a little less like my grandmother's name, because I'm named after my grandmother, both my grandmothers, Eilleen and Regina. I think, in my mind, I was just not really wanting to be called my grandmother's name onstage, so I decided to change it to Shania Twain. I met somebody with the name Shania, thought it was beautiful, and Shania Twain was born."

The “Good Luck, Babe!” singer’s onstage persona—Chappell Roan—has always been a “drag project,” allowing her to set boundaries and separate her personal life from the industry.

Oddly enough, Gigi Hadid got her stage name in school when the teacher would confuse her and a girl named Helena. Since her mom called her "gigi" as a term of endearment at home, she told the teacher to just call her Gigi and it simply stuck.

If you're looking for an explosive action-star name, how about Vin Diesel instead?

Miley Cyrus' bright smile is what inspired her childhood nickname, Smiley. Eventually the name was shortened to Miley and it stuck.

Apparently the singer looked more like a Bruno than a Peter, so one day his dad started calling him Bruno. Ever since, he has been known as Bruno Mars.

Before the meat dresses and chart-topping music, Lady Gaga was just an everyday girl from New York.

Jason Sudeikis confirmed on Today that he was named Daniel after his father, but following some confusion, his mom started using his middle name Jason to avoid confusion.

Emma Stone ended adopting this stage name after finding out that the Screen Actors Guild already had a member registered under her birthname.

Turns out Reese isn't even Reese Witherspoon's middle name. The actress chose the moniker in honor of her mother, whose maiden name is Reese.

Every artist has an alter ego, and Lana Del Rey just happens to be the persona of choice for the singer.

In 2014, Frank Ocean legally changed his name because, why not?

A little known fact about 30 Rock's Tina Fey is that she is actually named Elizabeth. The comedian made a not so subtle tribute to her birth name by naming her character Liz Lemon.

This pioneer of West Coast rap would later go on to be known as Snoop Dogg.

Turns out the "Royals" singer is really into learning about the aristocracy, hence the reason why she chose the name Lorde, but with a feminine 'e.'

Before he started shaking his bonbon for the masses, music superstar Ricky Martin went by this everyday name.

It's hard to believe that Iggy Azalea was born Amethyst Kelly!

If you’re looking to upgrade your headphones before the big wave of Black Friday deals arrives, you can buy JBL’s Tour One M3 for a new low price of $149 ($300 off) at Amazon, Best Buy, and directly from JBL. The deal is so good that my colleague John Higgins, The Verge’s audio and TV reviewer, popped in on his day off to tell us just how much of a steal they are at this price.

JBL’s Tour One M3 stand out largely because they come with the Smart Tx wireless audio transmitter. Frequent fliers will likely appreciate the accessory, which plugs into a plane’s in-flight entertainment system via a 3.5mm cable, allowing you to listen to audio wirelessly. It also works with other devices that lack Bluetooth support but have a headphone jack. The small touchscreen-equipped transmitter also lets you easily adjust the headphones’ noise cancellation, EQ settings, and volume. It can be used to answer calls, too.

Fancy tricks aside, the headphones get the essentials right. According to John, they offer excellent passive noise isolation, and their active noise cancellation is good enough to block out low-frequency sounds, like airplane engines. He also praises their overall sound quality, though he notes that JBL’s default tuning slightly affects midrange clarity, something you can easily fix by adjusting the EQ. They also support high-resolution lossless audio over the included USB-C or 3.5mm cable, plus spatial audio with head tracking. JBL says they can last up to 70 hours on a single charge with noise cancellation off — enough to get you through multiple long-haul flights — while the USB-C-chargeable transmitter offers up to 18 hours of battery life.

Kate Middleton’s Awkward Moment as a Fan Encourages Her to Break Protocol Ahead of the Earthshot Awards

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The royal had to stick to her guns and wouldn’t back down.

The Princess of Wales, best known as Kate Middleton, had a slightly awkward moment on her tour of India this week (where she’s raising awareness for her husband Prince William’s Earthshot Prize Awards, which reward innovative environmental solutions). While greeting an excited crowd gathered in Mumbai, the Princess sadly had to disappoint a cluster of children who were hoping to get her autograph.

Speaking to one child who had a pen and paper in hand for the Princess, Catherine reportedly had to say, “Sorry, I can’t!” as to sign her name would be a severe breach of royal protocol.

None of the royal family are allowed to give out autographs, owing to fears that their signature could be replicated and forged, or that they’ll simply be sold on for monetary gain.

In recent years, they have become more amenable to people requesting selfies, which previously was not common practice.

It’s far from the first time the Princess has had to navigate this type of delicate situation with a member of the public; earlier this year while on a visit to Wales, she was also asked to sign her name and had to offer up a consolatory handshake instead.

The trip to India is Kate’s first international visit since her cancer diagnosis in 2024, and her attendance was somewhat unexpected. She has missed the last three Earthshot Prize ceremonies, but given she’s traveled all the way to Mumbai, it’s not a surprise that she showed up alongside William for this year’s event, wearing a stunning Anarkali by Indian designer Mahima Mahajan.

The 2026 Earthshot Prize ceremony will not air live but will be available to watch on Amazon Prime from November 30.

Packers players will get their mandatory four days off after Week 12 game

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Packers have a bye in Week 11. Under the Collective Bargaining Agreement, the players are entitled to four consecutive days off.

Coach Matt LaFleur told reporters on Monday that, after discussions with the NFL Players Association, the Packers have decided to give their four days off following the first game after the bye.

It makes sense; there are fewer total days between the Packers’ Week 10 game and their Week 12 game (10) than there are between the Week 12 game and the Week 13 game (11).

The situation flows directly from the decision to play a Wednesday game, on the night before Thanksgiving. It removes the traditional two-week break, cobbling together a pair of mini-byes.

For the players, the break likely will coincide with Thanksgiving weekend, possibly giving them Thursday, Friday, Saturday, and Sunday off.

The Rams, in contrast, will surely be taking their four-day break before the Week 12 Wednesday night game. They play their Week 13 game against the Chiefs only eight days later, on the following Thursday night.

Mass Effect Spiritual Successor Exodus Has Revealed Its Version Of FemShep And I Have A Couple Of Questions

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It feels reductive to continually refer to Exodus, the new game from Archetype Entertainment developed by a team led by ex-BioWare developers, as a spiritual successor to Mass Effect. But it’s hard to stop calling it that when the game keeps reminding me of its now-deceased inspiration by calling the newly revealed female version of its main character FemJun.

WATCH the FemJun Reveal Trailer now! â¤µï¸

— EXODUS (@exodusgameofficial.bsky.social) 2026-11-17T15:12:37.746Z

Calling the female version of your default character “Fem+name” feels like an obvious throwback to Mass Effect and the way the community and developers alike referred to the female version of Commander ShepardÂ  as FemShep. It’s nice to be reminded of the good old days when FemShep, through the incredible performance of Jennifer Hale, became the community’s preferred version of the character (with no disrespect to the equally memorable performance of Mark Meer). But with Exodus so far removed in both time and development studio from Mass Effect, I wonder why such a distinction is even necessary. After all, the male version of Jun isn’t called MaleJun on Exodus‘ social channels, he’s just Jun.

// INCOMING SIGNALâ¦ :: CREW FILE: JUN ASLAN >> APPEARANCE: UPDATEDâ¦

— EXODUS (@exodusgameofficial.bsky.social) 2026-10-22T16:25:51.440Z

Why are video games doing that in 2026? Why was the female version of Jun teased out like this anyway? I remember being mightily concerned that there wouldn’t be the option to play as a female character when I was first digging into Exodus. Though brief mentions of her were buried in text, she didn’t show up in any earlier videos discussing the game or its characters. Maybe the developers hadn’t finalized her look until now or were hoping that, by saving her appearance for a grand announcement like this, they could make some of the sheen of FemShep rub off on FemJun by association. Appending “Fem” to Jun feels necessary for differentiation, but it also sets up maleness as the default and everything else a deviation.

Speaking of default: Are video game developers aware that, when making a game with a customizable character, there are other ways for that default character to look besides white? Because I’m not sure they are. The default look for both Jun characters seems to be white, adding to a long and storied tradition of video games setting up white as the standard, and that’s really something we don’t need to be doing in 2026. Character creators, presumably including the one Exodus will feature, have gotten so sophisticated, with a wide variety of different hair textures, eye colors, and skin colors to choose from. Why not pay just a little bit of homage to that wealth of creative options by putting forth a character that actually makes interesting use of it? Players, when they get their hands on this game, will be able to create a character to their tastes anyway.

None of this surprises me, nor am I looking for Exodus to be some kind of “woke” standard bearer among video games. But these questions are worth asking, sad as it is that they have to keep being asked, in the hopes that maybe, perhaps by the time the events of Exodus are meant to take place, things will have changed.

Jennifer Lopez Pours Us a Straight Dose of Holiday Cheer With Her Parade of Festive Fashion

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jennifer Lopez will never enter a room quietly. The “Proof of Love” singer is one for maximalist luxury—with her collection of Hermès bags, sky-high stilettos, shimmery catsuits, and fur coats—and now that the air has turned crisp, she’s doing holiday dressing the J.Lo way.

This week, the superstar has been in New York City promoting her concert special “The JLo Show: Live in Las Vegas” which premieres on November 26, as well as her new holiday collection of JLo Beauty Beso Balms. And if anyone knows how to dress for a promotional tour, it’s Lopez.

On Sunday, she kicked things off strong with a head-to-toe crimson look. A sheer candy apple-red pussy-bow blouse was tucked into silky matching shorts and layered under a cherry leather trench. For even more drama, Lopez slipped into a pair of red flower-print tights and rouge snakeskin pumps with golden stilettos. A Tom Ford alligator clutch in a deep apple red added the final monochrome touch, while diamond jewelry brought the shimmer to the Christmas-core ensemble.

Also on Sunday, Lopez changed into a sandy suede Retrofête minidress and chocolate pumps for an appearance on The Tonight Show Starring Jimmy Fallon. And she did several festive quick changes while on stage for the “Wig Swap Holiday Lounge Singers” bit.

Today, to attend Sirius XM’s Front Row, J.Lo wore a very different, but equally holiday-appropriate look. Leaning intro the traditional colors of the Christmas season—red and green—Lopez wore a sheer green top tucked into sheer burgundy tights and layered it all with mixed-metal chain belts.

An embellished cinnamon-brown jacket pulled the look together. And for a dose of jewel tones, Lopez popped on a pair of suede grape-colored heels (which matched her underwear) and carried a satin turquoise bag.

A shaggy black faux-fur coat from Auter kept her warm as she stepped outside the studio.

Most recently, on Instagram, the Office Romance star debuted what is maybe her most festive look of the week. A fluffy white fur coat from Valentino that was belted at the waist with a black leather bow and paired with black accessories: tights, pointy heels, a clutch, and oversize sunglasses. Oh, and don’t forget the extravagant diamond jewelry from the Back Vault x David Webb.

It seems it’s time to get out that holiday wardrobe, and we all better step it up.

PlayStation Has No GOTY Nominee at The Game Awards for First Time in 12 Years

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For the first time in 12 years — since all the way back in 2014 — there is no Sony-published PlayStation title amongst The Game Awards' Game of the Year nominees.

In that time, Sony has won three Game of the Year awards at Geoff's annual show, making it the most successful publisher in the event's history.

But its over-a-decade streak of nominations will finally come to an end here in 2026, as none of its releases from this year have made the cut.

That includes God of War: Sons of Sparta, Marathon, MLB The Show 26, Saros, Marvel Tokon: Fighting Souls, and Marvel's Wolverine.

In particular, Saros and Marvel Tokon got really strong reviews, but given just how stacked this year's competition is, it's no real surprise to see these relatively niche titles be left in the cold.

At the start of the year, many would have probably predicted that Wolverine would be in the mix — but a lukewarm critical reception has ultimately sealed its fate.

I don't think anyone would deny that Sony and its PlayStation Studios have had an off year. While their output has actually been quite frequent, none of their latest releases have made a lasting impact in the way that many of the above titles did in their respective years.

Elizabeth Debicki Thanks the People Who Opened Doors for Her in Her ELLE Women in Hollywood Speech

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Elizabeth Debicki knows that no career is built alone. On Monday night, the actress accepted her honor at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration, taking a moment to recognize the people who helped make her career possible.

After being introduced by Alexander Skarsgård, Debicki thanked her fellow honorees before reflecting on the support she has received throughout her journey.

“I really love to work. It is my great love,” she said. “And standing here tonight, I feel a really deep sense of gratitude for all the people—the small army of people—who have fought for me to be able to do my work.”

Debicki recalled how casting director Ronna Kress helped her secure an audition for Baz Luhrmann’s The Great Gatsby, which became her breakout film role. At the time, she was just 20 years old, had recently graduated from drama school in Australia, and had never done a screen test.

“Now, Baz, who is my fairy godfather, he fought very, very hard for me after I auditioned for him,” she explained. “But the only reason I was able to get that audition was because Ronna Kress watched the shoddy rookie tape I made back home and somehow managed to convince Baz to sit still long enough to watch it (which, if you know, you know is an actual miracle).”

She continued, “Ronna fought very, very hard for the studio to fly me out here to L.A.—I had never been here before—and to read for the part. And truly, that changed everything. That was really, really my door. And Ronna and Baz, they held that open for me.”

Debicki also praised the other women being recognized that evening: Julianne Moore, Penélope Cruz, Florence Pugh, Inde Navarette, Rosalind Eleazar, and Carrie Coon.

“In your work, your audiences are so privileged to witness your talent and your intelligence,” she said. “And this generosity in the way that you work, it makes us feel sane, and it makes us feel seen. So thank you, so, so much.”

Carrie Coon Speaks Out About Women’s Representation at ELLE’s Women in Hollywood Event

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Carrie Coon had a question for her fellow Women in Hollywood honorees: “Can we start a studio?”

On Monday night, the actress took the stage at ELLE’s 2026 Women in Hollywood celebration in Los Angeles, where she called for women in the entertainment industry to come together and create more opportunities for themselves.

Coon was introduced by her Gilded Age co-star Louisa Jacobson, who celebrated her recent career achievements.

“Carrie is a crusader, always fighting tirelessly for artistic integrity in a landscape that seems to value high gloss and profit over honest, nuanced storytelling,” Jacobson said. “And if you know Carrie, you know that she is completely allergic to superficiality.”

In her acceptance speech, Coon reflected on the importance of women’s stories, drawing inspiration from feminist literature and End Times Fascism, a book by Naomi Klein and Astra Taylor.

“Patriarchy, capitalism...these are hierarchical systems of domination that rely on fear,” she said. “And in this paradigm, women and girls, like our natural resources, are a disposable commodity—and as such, according to philosopher Kate Manne—our stories are interchangeable and of equal power. But of course, they’re not. That’s not true. And we’re not a monolith.”

Coon went on to discuss the performances of her fellow honorees, arguing that their work represented something extraordinary. “In these women, I see the fight for the living world. I see moral clarity. I see imagination. I see courage,” she said. “And these are, in fact, the antidotes to fascism. That’s how important this storytelling is.”

Toward the end of her speech, Coon questioned how women in Hollywood could create lasting change beyond events celebrating their individual achievements.

“How do we forge our collective safety and solidarity in an ethic of communalism?” she asked. “Okay, so this is the part of the speech where I don’t actually have the answer to that question, but I make the appeal that nights like these shouldn’t be one-off nights.”

She concluded with the aforementioned proposition for her peers: “And so I’m going to start with a really stupid question, but I’m not afraid to ask a stupid question: Can we start a studio?”

Watch Coon’s full speech above and read her Women in Hollywood cover story here.

9 Classics Where the Audiobook Version Is Better

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Audiobooks were technically born as far back as 1877, with Thomas Edison's invention of the phonograph. However, it wasn't until 1932 that the American Foundation for the Blind officially named them "Talking Books," introducing them as a medium for visually impaired people to enjoy literature as an alternative to Braille. Throughout the entire 20th century, and even well into the 21st, many classics have been turned into audiobooks so phenomenal that they're arguably even better than the original book.

Audiobooks and physical books are, of course, vastly different media that fulfill vastly different purposes, so comparing them can feel a bit like comparing apples to oranges. Still, sometimes, an audiobook is so good that it becomes entirely reasonable to say it provides a better experience than the original. These are audiobooks that anyone who enjoys classic literature should consider essential listening material. "Classic" is defined for the purposes of this list as any book that was originally published before the 21st century.

Folkloric vampires predate Bram Stoker's Dracula by hundreds of years. Inspired by such stories, as well as by Irish folklore and historical figures like Vlad the Impaler, Stoker wrote the 1897 classic that is nowadays widely remembered as the most foundational pillar of modern vampiric fiction. It's undoubtedly one of the best horror books of all time, which made the announcement of an original Audible audiobook-slash-audio-drama all the more worthy of skepticism.

In the end, however, Bram Stoker's Dracula ended up being one of the greatest horror audiobooks that readers have been treated to in years. With a stacked cast that includes the likes of Ella Purnell, Jonathan Bailey, and Aimee Lou Wood, the audiobook takes the classic epistolary novel and makes it feel even more immersive, cinematic, and well-paced. It's worth noting that it's an abridged audio drama rather than a full reading of the original novel, which is not ideal, but those interested in such a concept should love it.

Originally serialized between 1875 and 1877, Russian author Leo Tolstoy's Anna Karenina is widely regarded as one of the most foundational pieces of world literature in history, a must-read masterpiece whose daunting 1,000-page length shouldn't deter anyone from considering it essential reading. Since it was one of her favorite books of all time, Maggie Gyllenhaal agreed to narrate an Audible original audiobook that really does justice to one of the best epic books of all time.

Tolstoy's novel was such a monumental artistic achievement that the author was notoriously drained and exhausted by the whole experience by the time he was done, and reading such a vast book can itself also be a massive undertaking. With the raw immediacy and many emotional variations of Gyllenhaal's narration, listening to Anna Karenina can be a far more engaging and far less tedious experience than many may initially think. At over 30 hours long, it's definitely a time commitment, but oh so worth every minute.

Upon publication back in 1997, J. K. Rowling's Harry Potter and the Philosopher's Stone (published in the US as Harry Potter and the Sorcerer's Stone) was an immediate commercial and critical hit, kicking off one of the biggest multimedia franchises in history. The book's first audiobook version came out in 1999, and the performers varied by region. Whereas Jim Dale narrated the book in the US and Canada, it was Stephen Fry's voice that was heard in the British and international releases. If there is any one Harry Potter book that's better heard than read, it's Fry's performance of Philosopher's Stone.

As a whole, this is one of the most essential fantasy books to read before kicking the bucket, but there's nothing quite like hearing it through Fry's rich, velvety baritone voice. On the page, Philosopher's Stone is beautifully imaginative and cozy, but at times feels like an overly simple debut novel in terms of Rowling's prose. Fry elevates every word and makes the entire story feel like a wonderful fairy tale being told by the fire on a cold weekend night outdoors.

One of Stephen King's first novels was 1983's Pet Sematary, published before he had fully established and cemented himself as the true King of Horror. Fans had been begging for an audiobook for years, and once the project finally materialized, it was Michael C. Hall of Dexter fame who publisher Simon & Schuster picked as the right voice to narrate the story. The result is one of the best Stephen King audiobooks to date, and considering just how many great ones there are, that's saying a lot.

Pet Sematary was already absolutely legendary well before 2018, widely regarded by many—including King himself—as the author's most genuinely terrifying work. However, once the horror aspects really pick up steam after a relatively slow-burning first few pages, Hall makes it hit even harder. Getting an actor well-known for projects as death-related as Six Feet Under and Dexter to narrate a horror novel all about death and grief was not only brilliant meta-casting, but also hugely beneficial for the story as a whole.

Multiple books are almost always brought up when the endless debate of what "The Great American Novel" is gets brought up, but John Steinbeck's East of Eden is the most noteworthy. This family saga was likely key to Steinbeck being awarded the Nobel Prize in Literature in 1962, and it more than lives up to its reputation as one of the most perfect American books ever written. The first audiobook version of the classic ever recorded was narrated by respected character actor Richard Poe.

East of Eden was already widely recognized as one of the most perfectly written masterpieces of all time, and it's definitely a must-read, but there's something about the way Poe complements the narrative that makes his version arguably even more unforgettable. Steinbeck wrote East of Eden in such a sweeping, almost Biblical voice that it almost feels like the narration of a legend, and Poe's resonant yet deeply human voice allows that narration style to feel all the more intimate and personal.

The book that earned Harper Lee a Pulitzer Prize was 1960's To Kill a Mockingbird, one of the most essential and widely discussed American classics of the 20th century. Though certain elements of its narrative are undeniably dated by modern standards, its heart will forever be in the right place, with its critiques of racial prejudice and systemic injustice being as timely and hard-hitting today as they were back in the '60s. In 2014, legendary actress Sissy Spacek was tasked with narrating the audiobook version of the story.

The reasons why this is one of those great books that are even better as audiobooks are aplenty, and even though the Pulitzer-winning original remains absolutely foundational, Spacek brings a level of nuance and authenticity to the book that feels like a much-welcome sense of modernity. The actress' beautifully melodic Texan drawl makes the small-town Alabama setting feel all the more evocative and atmospheric, and the way she flawlessly captures Scout's personality as both a child and an adult is incredible.

Stephen King's 22nd book was 1986's It, still remembered by many as the author's best and most iconic work to date. At over 1,100 pages in length, it's an absolute brick of a horror novel, but its epic scope still works marvelously all these many years later. To narrate the 2016 audiobook, Simon & Schuster decided to go for an actor who already had a pre-existing tie to the Stephen King universe: Steven Weber, who played Jack Torrance in the 1997 three-part miniseries version of The Shining.

It was already one of those horror books where every chapter is perfect—something quite admirable for such a thick novel—but Weber took that literary doorstop and somehow turned it into an even bigger, more immersive 45-hour juggernaut of an audiobook. The actor gives each of the Losers a distinct voice both in their child and adult versions, and he goes all in on Pennywise without ever teetering too far into distracting camp. Perfectly paced and intense to the core, It is a must-listen-to audiobook through and through.

American poet, essayist, and civil rights activist Maya Angelou was a towering figure in modern American literature, completely reshaping how Black American identity was expressed in the written word. The first of her seven published memoirs, 1969's I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings, broke new ground and became one of the first major autobiographies by an African American woman to achieve global bestseller status. When an audiobook version was released in 2011, it was Angelou herself who narrated it.

Taking one of the best memoirs of all time and turning it into an audiobook worthy of its legacy was always going to be a tough task, so who better to achieve it than the author herself? By having Angelou narrate her own story with her unmistakably theatrical contralto voice, I Know Why the Caged Bird Sings feels even more honest and intimate than it already does on the page, balancing poignancy and humor to absolute perfection.

Well before he wrote what many still consider the greatest and most influential fantasy epic of the 20th century, J. R. R. Tolkien published the fantasy children's classic The Hobbit, almost inarguably the most perfect fantasy novel of the 1930s. There are multiple audiobook versions of The Hobbit available, but one reigns supreme as perhaps the audiobook that most elevates the experience of the original text: the 2020 version narrated by Andy Serkis.

It's easily one of the best audiobooks of all time, with Serkis not only delivering a much-expected powerhouse performance as Gollum, but also giving each of the other characters a distinct voice that makes them even more memorable than they are on paper. The Hobbit is also filled with songs and poems, and Serkis brings them to life in a way that reading the physical book simply can't match. In Serkis' voice, The Hobbit really does feel like the wonderful myth that it was always meant to be.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Brian Flores: Vikings plan to rotate players in order to deal with altitude in Mexico City

📅 2026-11-17  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 49ers are spending the week in Colorado in order to acclimate to the altitude at which their Week 11 game in Mexico City will be played. The Vikings are not.

But the Vikings have a plan for ensuring that their players are ready for the conditions.

“It’s a tough place to play,” Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores told reporters on Tuesday regarding the game in Mexico. “I think it’s certainly taxing, you know, I think you’re — I think as a staff, we’re already talking about, ‘Hey, we’re gonna have to rotate these guys and get, you know, everybody’s gonna have to be ready to play.’ And so, yeah, I mean, it’s certainly something that we’re going to have to deal with.

“But there’s always, you know, elements that we’re dealing with, whether it’s cold or rain or things like that. Obviously, we’re indoors, so we don’t deal with much of that. But that’s the environment we’re gonna be in. I think we prepare them to play in that environment, but I’m not gonna be silly and say, ‘Hey, everybody, hey, you’ve got to go in there for 12 straight plays.’ I mean, we’re not doing that.

“So we’ll have rotations, we’ll have a plan in place. Inevitably, we’re gonna have to fight through some of that. It’s not gonna be your average game where, you know, altitude, you just have — you literally have less oxygen. So we’ve got to do a great job as a staff of making sure — watching guys, and making sure we got guys in and out, you know, as much as we can.”

His broader message to the defense is clear: “We’re gonna need everybody.”

Regardless of the plan to rotate players, the question becomes whether the 49ers will be able to rely more heavily on their best players because they’re properly acclimated to the thinner air. The Vikings are choosing a different strategy, and it’s going to create an obvious apples to apples comparison between the approaches that the two teams have taken — especially if the 49ers players seem to be fresh and the Vikings players seem to be gassed.

Gael Cameron Says Her ‘Below Deck’ Romance With Nathan Gallagher Was Abusive: ‘I Am Still Scared’

📅 Nov 17, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Almost a year after Below Deck star Nathan Gallagher was arrested for domestic abuse in Australia — and 12 weeks after the news went public — Gallagher’s former partner and cast member Gael Cameron is finally speaking out about their relationship.

Cameron broke her silence on an episode of the podcast We Need To Talk, hosted by Paul C. Brunson, where she publicly accused Gallagher of abuse for the first time and described a toxic relationship that’s left her in fear to this day.

“I know that he is not afraid to do anything to throw me under. He’s not afraid to lie… He’s always had this idea that he has ownership or control over me,” Cameron said tearfully. “I still feel that. And I am still scared of him. And that’s why I’ve not said anything, even though he’s thrown all these accusations out there.”

Gallagher and Cameron met while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023, both working on the superyacht’s deck team. Over three seasons, their relationship evolved from flirty co-workers to welcoming a son together last June. But this August — halfway through a season where Gallagher struggled to juggle his bosun duties and be a present father — news broke of Gallagher’s December 2025 arrest on domestic violence charges. According to Australian police, Gallagher attacked Cameron at her apartment in Sydney, resulting in his arrest on Dec. 27, 2025. During the physical altercation, Gallagher “placed both of his hands around the victim’s neck applying enough pressure which made her unable to breathe,” according to police records. He was charged with assault occasioning actual bodily harm, common assault, and intentionally choking a person without consent.

Although Gallagher previously told Rolling Stone he had proof that would clear his name, he failed to appear at a mandated court hearing in September. As a result, he was convicted by default on charges of assault. Australian authorities issued a warrant for his arrest, noting Gallagher was suspected to have fled the country. Bravo has since announced it has no plans to work with him.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Both Bravo and Gallagher declined to comment further to Rolling Stone.

During the podcast episode, Cameron charts the timeline of her relationship with Gallagher, including the behind-the-scenes struggles that kept her silent and afraid. Cameron first met Gallagher while filming Season Nine of Below Deck: Mediterranean in 2023. At the time, she was dating another man but began a friendship with Gallagher. Their working relationship was often “playfully flirty” when the cameras were rolling, but they didn’t start seeing each other until filming wrapped.

In 2023, the two were in a long-distance relationship, but Cameron said there were early signs of anger issues in Gallagher. When they were able to meet up, Cameron described his “love bombing,” showering her with gifts and affection. When she found out she was pregnant, Gallagher convinced her to keep the baby — the first of many moves which she claims he did in order to isolate her.

“If I ever said, ‘I don’t know if I can do this. I don’t think it’s the right time,’ He would make me feel very bad about myself. He would make me feel very guilty and just terrible about myself,” she said. “I have quite a conservative mum. I couldn’t talk to anyone. So he was kind of my only sounding board because I was so afraid to tell anyone.”

But the pregnancy only aggravated Gallagher’s behavior, according to Cameron. She said that she spent close to “90 percent” of her pregnancy in tears, often feeling like any small mistake would trigger Gallagher’s anger. In one instance, she describes that while drunk, Gallagher jumped out of a moving car rather than talk to her. Once a social worker told her the relationship was “unsafe,” Cameron began reevaluating if they could stay together long term. “I’d be told to shut the fuck up. I’d be called a bitch, a slut, and all these horrible things,” she says. “There were multiple times when I was like ‘No no no, this cannot continue,’ because my son cannot grow up thinking that this is how you speak to women.” But then Below Deck came to call.  Related Content Hayden Panettiere's Mother Blames Brian Hickerson For Star’s Death Kid Rock's Nashville Bar Sued After Bouncers Allegedly Beat Patron Who 'Tapped' Guitar Lawsuit Accusing Selena Gomez and Wondermind Co-Founders of Fraud Is Dismissed Priscilla Presley Scores Partial Win Over Fraud Suit, But Claims About Lisa Marie’s Death Can Stand

When Gallagher flew to Croatia to film Below Deck: Mediterranean Season 11, Cameron didn’t initially want to go. She appeared on the series as a guest star, and lived off of the boat with her infant son. But she believes Gallagher pushed her to come to Croatia as another form of control. “It felt as though he was very worried about who I spoke to or who I was around when he wasn’t around,” she says. “There was this element of control that he would lose over me if I wasn’t in the same country as him.” She also describes incidents during heated fights, when Gallagher would record on his phone, pretending that she was striking him or hitting him unprovoked. Things came to a head on Dec. 27, 2025, when Cameron said Gallagher choked her while their son was in her arms, which led to Gallagher’s arrest and subsequent conviction. Cameron appeared visibly emotional when she recounted the night.

“When I was in the bathroom, and he had his hands around my neck and attempted to lift, my son was in my arms. All I could think is, ‘If I fall, so does he.’ From that moment, I should have known there’s no return,” she says. “When he had me pinned down and he was striking me on the jaw, I actually sat there and I asked if he was going to kill me. There was a moment where I actually asked that question. The reason I say this is because if things had not gotten that bad, I would never have gotten out.
