Blade Runner 2099 bosses hint at a possible "triple-A" Blade Runner game in the future - just don't expect it any time soon

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Blade Runner 2099 - an upcoming TV miniseries coming to Amazon Prime Video, acting as a sequel to Blade Runner (1982) and Blade Runner 2049 (2017) - might be getting a video game. And a 'triple-A' video game, at that. At least that's according to Andrew Kosove and Broderick Johnson, executive producers for Blade Runner 2099 and co-CEOs of Alcon Entertainment (which owns the Blade Runner IP).

In a new interview with IGN, the duo reveal that they had thought about adapting the Blade Runner fracnhise into a game, and that - should it come to fruition - it would target "triple-A" in scope. Other than that, the duo seemed to want to keep any potential information to themselves. Given they appear to be in talks about the title, rather than in any active development, if something did come of this ideation, it would no doubt be a long, long way off. Kosove and Johnson's aim seems to be getting players to "interact" more with the world of Blade Runner, which has recently been fleshed out by the 2017 movie and is set to be developed more in the upcoming series.

Of course, we've seen Blade Runner games before. There was 1997's simply-titled Blade Runner - developed by Westwood Studios and published by Virgin Interactive - which was a point-and-click adventure that really seemed to pick up the vibes the original Ridley Scott was putting down. An Enhanced Edition of the game was released in 2022, which is actually more of a remake than a simple enhancement.

Then, in 2023, Annapurna Interactive unveiled its very first internally-developed game, in the form of Blade Runner 2033: Labyrinth. The game, which promised to riff on the dystopian universe established in Ridley Scott's cult classic movie, would have been sandwiched squarely between that film and its sequel, Blade Runner 2049. It was going to be set in LA after the Blackout hits, but beyond that - and the fact it's coming to PC and consoles at some point - few details ever managed to break through the PR barrier before the game went dark: it's been removed from Annapurna's website and delisted from Steam. Richard Sterban, Oak Ridge Boys’ Bass Singer, Dies at 83

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sterban died Nov. 14 in Hendersonville, Tennessee, due to complications from bronchitis and pneumonia after fighting a battle with cancer, his family and the music group announced.

Richard Sterban, the Oak Ridge Boys’ bass singer who memorably lent his vocals to their beloved “Elvira” cover, has died. He was 83.

Sterban died Nov. 14 in Hendersonville, Tennessee, due to complications from bronchitis and pneumonia after fighting a battle with cancer, his family and the Oak Ridge Boys announced.

“We appreciate all of your love, support, and prayers. Please continue to lift us up in this difficult time, we find comfort knowing that he is currently face to face with Jesus,” Sterban’s family said in a statement. Related Stories TV Dawn Greenhalgh, Voice of the Elephant Queen Celeste in 'Babar' Cartoons, Dies at 93 Music Gerry Griffith, Music Executive Who Discovered Whitney Houston, Dies at 87

Born April 24, 1943, in Camden, New Jersey, Sterban began singing at six in Sunday school as a soprano. He sang tenor in his seventh-grade glee club, and upon his eighth grade, his voice dropped to bass.

He studied music at Trenton State College (modernly known as the College of New Jersey), and began singing in a gospel quartet where he met Joe Bonsall, his future Oak Ridge Boys bandmate.

While singing part-time, Sterban joined J.D. Sumner and the Stamps, whom Elvis Presley hired to sing behind him. He then sang and recorded with the King of Rock and Roll, and also appeared in the 1972 concert film Elvis on Tour.

Sterban was then offered the opportunity to join the Oak Ridge Boys after the departure of Noel Fox, which he accepted in 1972. Bonsall joined the group one year later, with Duane Allen, William Lee Golden and Sterban already in the group, which carried on as the faces of the band for five decades.

They performed with Johnny Cash, who helped them sign with Columbia Records in 1973, though it was after opening for Roy Clark in 1975 when they met his manger, Jim Halsey, who would later sign with the group.

They reached success with “Y’all Come Back Saloon,” which reached No. 3 on the country chart in 1977, and led to a string of hits. “I’ll Be True to You” became their first No. 1 song, though 1981’s “Elvira” would become one of their largest successes.

Originally recorded by Dallas Frazier, the Oak Ridge Boys’ version of the track went to No. 1 on the country chart and No. 5 on the pop chart, sold more than two million copies and won a Grammy. Sterban’s bass refrain was a major touchpoint on the song.

The Oak Ridge Boys went on to also become members of the Grand Ole Opry, the Gospel Music Hall of Fame and the Country Music Hall of Fame.

Sterban released his memoir From Elvis to Elvira: My Life on Stage, written with Steven Robinson, in 2012.

Sterban is survived by his wife, Donna; his daughters, Lauren (Tyler) and Tori (Michael); his sons, Rich (Chele), Doug, and Chris; his grandchildren, Mali (Zachary), Nikolas, Tyler (Ryann), Madelyn, Alexandra, Rex, Owen, and Charli Grace; and his great-grandchildren, Salem, Asher, and Theo. He was preceded in death by his mother Victoria Sterban, brother Joseph Sterban and grandson Zachary.

The nationwide ground stop that shut down the aviation system has impacted the NFL’s officiating assignments for Sunday games. And while the league has announced the specific changes that will result in non-referees serving as referees for four games on Sunday (with side judge Don Willard working two games in the same day), other adjustments have not been officially announced.

There surely will be others. For now, one game will be shorthanded.

Via Tom Pelissero of Netflix, the Panthers-Saints game won’t have a replay assistant.

Those duties will be handled by the league office. Which will put a strain on the usual resources there, especially in the cluster of 1:00 p.m. ET games. (Panthers-Saints is one of the seven games to be played in that window.)

Given that the league has otherwise adjusted the Week 10 assignments to keep the officials within driving distance of their hometowns, it’s likely that the existing crews will be altered significantly. Which will introduce unfamiliarity and otherwise complicate the effort to get calls right on a consistent basis.

Absent an advance announcement from the league as to the full extent of the changes, they won’t be known until the NFL posts the official Game Books for the Sunday contests.

John Wick Faces Off Against Ballerina in This Invasion Thriller Officially Leaving HBO Max

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. Years before they shared the screen in From the World of John Wick: Ballerina, Ana de Armas and Keanu Reeves starred together in an erotic exploitation movie directed by Eli Roth. A friend and collaborator of Quentin Tarantino, Roth had already established his genre credentials with the horror film Hostel and the cannibal survival thriller The Green Inferno — both are regarded as some of the most transgressive movies made in mainstream Hollywood. Roth's home invasion thriller with De Armas and Reeves wasn't as edgy, although it was still shocking enough to remain in its niche. The film in question is currently streaming on HBO Max in the United States, giving fans of genre cinema a great opportunity to check it out.

It won't, however, be available for much longer. The movie was released a year after the first John Wick, which emerged as a surprise hit and reinvigorated Reeves' career as an action star. The rest is history, with the John Wick franchise having generated more than $1 billion in global box-office revenue in the decade since. Reeves is set to return as the titular assassin in a fifth film, following his extended cameo in Ballerina, which was released last year.

Ballerina marked De Armas' first time leading a potential franchise-starter of her own, after having delivered memorable performances in hits such as Knives Out, No Time to Die, and the Oscar-nominated Blonde. Despite mostly positive reviews, Ballerina underperformed at the box office, grossing around $140 million against a reported budget of $90 million. De Armas wasn't a household name back in 2015, when she first worked with Reeves in the home invasion thriller.

Four franchises, four completely different ways of getting underneath your skin. This is the one built out of your particular fear, why it works so well on you, and what it would leave behind.

Your nightmare does not wait outside the door. It waits for you to close your eyes, which you are going to have to do eventually, because nobody has ever won that particular argument.

Your nightmare is not really the doll. It is the face of the person you tell, and the way they decide, very kindly, that you have imagined all of it.

Your nightmare has your number, knows your name, and has almost certainly been inside your house before. The worst part is that you already know them.

Your nightmare is not really about you. It is about the people asleep down the hall, and the feeling that whatever is here is not interested in leaving.

We're talking, of course, about Knock Knock. The movie follows two young women who show up at the doorstep of an architect (Reeves) and coerce him into committing a sexual transgression. Soon, the architect finds himself at their mercy, as they hold him hostage in his house. The movie received mixed reviews and is now sitting at a 37% score on Rotten Tomatoes, where the consensus reads, "Knock Knock brings a lot of talent to bear on its satirical approach to torture horror, but not effectively enough to overcome its repetitive story or misguidedly campy tone."

Audiences were apparently not on the same page either, awarding it a poor 19% score on the aggregator website. Knock Knock grossed around $6 million at the box office against a reported budget of $10 million. Meryl Streep, Jon Hamm, Dave Franco Brought Comedy To ‘Hoppers’, Director Daniel Chong Says – Contenders L.A.

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Pixar animated film Hoppers features an eclectic voice cast, from Meryl Streep and Jon Hamm to Dave Franco and Bobby Moynihan. Whatever their background, director Daniel Chong wanted funny people. At Contenders Film, Chong said comedy drove the casting.

“Comedy was the No. 1 thing we wanted as casting,” Chong said. “[Hamm’s] post-Mad Men career has been a lot of comedy. That guy is not afraid to look stupid. We definitely put him through it in the movie. Meryl was also down for jokes. She came in with that really weird voice. She came in hot with that voice and we were like okay, here we go.”

Hamm plays the mayor who is approving the razing of a glade. Meryl Streep plays the insect queen. In the film, young Mabel (Piper Curda), uses Hopper technology to control a robot beaver. As a beaver, she rallies the animals to save the glade. Watch on Deadline

Franco plays Streep’s son and worked hard to keep up.

“With Dave, we also put him through the ringer because that is a very high octane performance,” Chong said. “He has to go to 11 every line. He was sweating after every take.”

Curda joined Chong and producer Nicole Paradis Grindle at Contenders. Curda was a TikToker, Chong discovered.

“That’s not why I made TikTok,” Curda said. “I was bored. Now I’m here.”

Mabel is a young environmentalist. Curda hopes her passion inspires young viewers.

“What’s so cool about Mabel is she’s not shy about really caring and also at the same time, she’s incredibly cool,” Curda said. “it’s such a cool takeaway, especially for a young person, to be like it’s cool to care. You can care a lot about something maybe most people don’t care about and still be awesome. I think it’s really cool to care.”

Grindle acknowledged there is a message to Hoppers. Comedy helps them deliver it, as she quoted another Disney classic.

“A spoonful of sugar makes the medicine go down,” Grindle said. “We’re all about the sugar. It’s more meaningful when it’s rooted in something deeper. You need to have that kind of emotional catharsis to come out of the movie.”

Every Saturday, plenty of players get fined for illegal use of helmet fouls from the prior weekend’s game. For Week 9, Texans safety Jalen Pitre got fined twice.

Both instances occurred in the second quarter of the victory over the Chargers. Pitre was penalized for neither infraction.

Each fine will cost $15,400, subject to appeal. They were Pitre’s first fines of the season.

For the season (including the preseason), 53 fines have been imposed for illegal use of the helmet.

The NFL will impose larger fines on Pitre for future violations. Eventually, he’ll be at risk for a suspension.

Use of the helmet isn’t one of the five safety rules that trigger a one-game suspension in the event of three violations.

5 Amazing Western Revenge Movies You Can Watch For Free

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

For those who enjoy watching hardened gunslingers shooting their way to revenge, there are plenty of worthwhile options that can be streamed for free. Tubi, a streaming service known for not requiring a paid subscription to watch its content, hosts a vast library of Western movies.

Vengeance is one of the most popular themes of Western films. It's the driving force of so many stories in the genre. Because of how common it can be, a long list of these Westerns proved to be rather forgettable, somewhat standard entries into the genre. Others, however, stood out from the crowd to join the ranks of the greatest Westerns of all time, a distinction earned by the likes of Unforgiven, Hang 'Em High, The Searchers, and For a Few Dollars More.

A few movies that belong in that same category can currently be streamed for free, thanks to Tubi. Beyond that, the streaming service also offers a number of lesser-known - but incredibly entertaining - Western movies that cast the spotlight on bitter gunslingers with a score to settle.

Released in 2003, Open Range stars Western icons Kevin Costner and Robert Duvall as aging ranchers who resist when a gang of thugs led by an unscrupulous cattle baron tries to bully them off the land in the Montana territory. Open Range starts out as a story of resistance against a powerful force in a Western town, but when the villains of the film ultimately take a step too far, it evolves into a brutal tale of revenge.

The mysterious past of Kevin Costner's character comes into play here, with his skills as a gunfighter being key to their efforts to fight back. Their determination to not only hold their own ground, but also get revenge for their fallen culminates in a strikingly gruesome grand finale. Unlike many of the Westerns of the previous era, Open Range pulls no punches when it comes to showing its characters dying in grisly fashion. But the violence is never gratitious; Open Range successfully presents itself as a dark but appropriately realistic take on life in the American Wild West.

With its gritty take on the Western formula and outstanding performances from its two lead actors, Open Range has a well-deserved reputation as one of the greatest Westerns of the 21st century thus far.

The Forgotten Pistolero is an underrated 1969 Spaghetti Western that places Leonard Mann in the role of Sebastian, a man who grew up with no knowledge of his true history. After getting dragged into a conflict with a pair of scheming criminals and their cronies, Sebastian learns the tragic details of his past, and the identies of those responsible. Teaming up with his friend, Rafael, Sebastian goes on a mission of revenge to get justice for his father and save his sister.

A big part of what makes The Forgotten Pistolero's revenge tale so interesting is the target of his crusade; Sebastian's own mother is one of the two main villains of the movie, setting up an emotional confrontation at the end of the story where the hero gets a chance to make her and her former lover pay for their crimes. If the plot sounds familiar, that's because The Forgotten Pistolero is essentially a loose adaptation of the Greek myth of Orestes, though done in the format of a Spaghetti Western. It's an unorthodox combination, but the concept makes for a compelling revenge film.

In 1971, Western heavyweight Burt Lancaster starred in Valdez is Coming. Lancaster plays a lawman named Valdez, who was deceived into killing someone in the movie's opening act. Shortly afterward, the man who perpetrated the deception used the power he holds in the town to have Valdez painfully humiliated and dragged out into the desert.

Unbeknownst to the villain, Burt Lancaster's Valdez is a seasoned sharpshooter, a background that is crucial to his plan of retaliation. Unwilling to forget what was done to him, Valdez meticulously hunts down his enemy's henchmen, one by one. Valdez's relentless killing spree continues until it builds into a dramatic showdown with his prime target.

Similar in some ways to Unforgiven, Valdez is Coming is a movie where the villains unwittingly invite disaster upon themselves by an old gunslinger they once deemed as a non-threat. Fear consumes them as Lancaster's character easily asserts control over the situation. He's on his own, but it's clearly the villains who have their backs against the wall.

For those who enjoy the one-man army aspect of both Western movies and modern-day revenge thrillers like John Wick, Valdez is Coming is a must-watch. And despite being in his late 50s at this point in his career, Burt Lancaster is still as imposing as ever, demonstrating his worth as one of the Western genre's best leading actors across its entire history in Hollywood.

Two years into the legendary run of the celebrated Western TV series Gunsmoke, the show's star - James Arness - played the lead role in Gun the Man Down, a significantly darker film set in the same genre. Rather than keeping the peace and maintaining law in order, Arness plays an outlaw with a serious grudge on his mind, and one whose vicious methods go a bit beyond the norm.

Gun the Man Down delivers a gritty take on the Western revenge trope that is likely to please fans of movies like Hang 'Em High and High Plains Drifter. In the movie, Arness' character is a criminal whose cohorts betrayed him and essentially left him holding the bag. But rather than simply exact revenge on them following his release from prison, he decides to drag out the process by terrorizing them emotionally, adding an interesting, psychological horror element to the Western.

In 1959's Ride Lonesome, Randolph Scott plays a bounty hunter named Ben Brigade, who captures a criminal (James Best) and sets out to deliver him to justice. But his efforts actually hide an ulterior motive; his real goal is to force out of hiding his prisoner's parter-in-crime, who is responsible for the death of Brigade's wife years earlier.

Ride Lonesome boasts a surprising amount of star power; in addition to having Randolph Scott in the lead role, the 1959 Western also includes bad guy roles for two of the Western genre's best villain actors in Lee Van Cleef and James Coburn, and naturally, neither disappoints. The sinister nature of Van Cleef's character only make Randolph Scott's hero even easier to root for.

Ride Lonesome is easily one of Randolph Scott's best Westerns, and that's saying a lot when taking into consideration that he was one of the most prolific and most popular actors associated with in the genre during the 1940s and 1950s.

Ewan McGregor’s ‘Jurassic Park’ Replacement Is Officially Taking Over Streaming

It's difficult to imagine a busier Hollywood star in 2026 than Anne Hathaway. Olivia Wilde Shares Rare Insight Into Coparenting With Jason Sudeikis

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Olivia Wilde shared rare comments on coparenting kids Otis, 12, and Daisy, 10, with ex Jason Sudeikis six years after their breakup: "We have reached this healthy equilibrium."

Olivia Wilde is extending an invite into her family dynamic.

Six years after the Don’t Worry Darling star’s breakup with Jason Sudeikis, she shared rare comments about their coparenting relationship as they raise their kids Otis, 12, and Daisy, 10.

“I really take pride in how good we are at coparenting our kids in my family,” Wilde shared on the Nov. 14 episode of the New York Times’ The Interview podcast. “We have reached this healthy equilibrium.”

Yet, she admitted that achieving this dynamic can be difficult.

“It’s so hard,” the Invite director continued. “Anyone who pretends it’s easy—like, ‘Oh, you just consciously uncouple, and then you find a way’—that’s bulls--t.”

And Wilde emphasized that she and Sudeikis make a point to always be honest with their kids.

“I feel relieved that I’m able to give them a life with parents where there’s a real sense of authenticity in terms of how everyone’s feeling and doing,” she explained. “What happens is when you’re trying to hide [interpersonal conflict] from your kids, they sense it. They’re the most intuitive people in the house.”

“People start to feel this sense of like, ‘What is real? ‘Cause I can tell that something's being performed for me, but I can sense it's not real,’” the 42-year-old added. “And that I wouldn’t wish on anyone.”

And though Wilde and Sudeikis—who called off their engagement in 2020 after nearly 10 years together—didn’t work out as a couple, the House alum stressed that it was ultimately the best decision for their kids.

“It feels like a failure when you can’t make a relationship work,” Wilde admitted, “particularly if it’s with the person you have children with.”

The I Want Your Sex star added that the breakup was “by far the most difficult, painful decision I’ve ever made in my life—to understand that it was not better for my kids to live in a house where their parents were not aligned.”

But despite Wilde and Sudeikis’ busy careers, the former pair always make showing up for their children their priority.

"I’m really grateful for the opportunity to have this space with my kids when I am completely with my kids, completely engaged with them,” Wilde shared during a June appearance on the Call Her Daddy podcast. “I really value the opportunity to be completely present with them and then to hand them off to a very capable coparent who is very present with them when he’s with them and then I have this space which is permission to go work my f--king ass off.”

Netflix Officially Brings Back a Hit 3-Part Rom-Com Series

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix has built a strong foundation of romance titles over the years. 2026 alone saw the return of some of their heaviest hitters, with Bridgerton returning for Season 4 towards the beginning of the year and Heartstopper officially wrapping up with the release of its feature-length finale, Heartstopper Forever. The teen drama My Life With the Walter Boys also welcomed viewers back to Silver Falls with Season 3, with plenty of swoon-worthy moments to boot. When it comes to rom-com series specifically, though, there's only one that everybody wants more of.

Netflix has officially renewed its red-hot story of star-crossed lovers for another run as of November 6. It's part of a surprising run of renewals for the streamer this month, alongside Adults, Youth, Outlast, and, in a history-maker for the platform, Virgin River, which will become its longest-running scripted drama by a number of seasons at nine. Nobody Wants This continuing is no shocker, given the viewership and Season 3 rating as its second-best on Rotten Tomatoes with a Certified Fresh score of 83% from critics. Erin Foster, Jenni Konner, and Bruce Eric Kaplan will be back as co-showrunners, writing, "We are so happy and thrilled to do another season of our favorite show on television, which coincidentally is the one we run."

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.

The intensity was never the problem. The secrecy is. You are drawn to a love that lives entirely in private, and to the particular ache of wanting somebody you are not supposed to want.

You do not do this cautiously. When it happens for you it is immediate and total and it rearranges everything, and you would not accept a smaller version of it.

Season 3 ended with wedding bells ringing for Noah and Joanne on the beach in a surprise ceremony in front of no one but God and a random man walking his dog nearby. What was initially meant as a trip to finalize Joanne's conversion to Judaism turns into a sacred moment for the couple that binds them together forever. However, they still have an entire squabbling family to break the news to, all of whom are sure to be very normal about it.

There's also the reason for their bickering to deal with — Sasha is caught between his estranged wife Esther and Morgan, with both finding out about his hook-ups with the other. His story is expected to get a big spotlight in Season 4, with all of Stephanie Faracy, Michael Hitchcock, Paul Ben-Victor, Tovah Feldshuh, Andrew Rannells, Sarah Silverman, Shiloh Bearman, and Sherry Cola likely returning alongside the aforementioned stars.

Contenders Hall Of Fame Honoree Peter Farrelly Talks ‘I Play Rocky’, ‘Dumb & Dumber’, The Hair Gel Bit In ‘There’s Something About Mary’ & More

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Prolific director, screenwriter and producer Peter Farrelly reflected on some of his most famous films Saturday at Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles event.

Contenders Hall of Fame honoree Farrelly talked with Deadline’s Mike Fleming Jr about his most recent film I Play Rocky and the process of bringing it to the screen. Earlier, Farrelly had appeared on the film’s panel with star Anthony Ippolito, who plays Sylvester Stallone in the film, and talked about the uphill battle to making the crowd-pleasing underdog tale.

Farrelly revealed that it was his daughter’s ex-boyfriend who first read the script and told Farrelly it “was the best script he had ever read.” Farrelly agreed and pushed forward with the film, noting it was imperative that he had Stallone’s approval on the script, which came quickly.

He also shared his own “Rocky” moment: “The bravest thing I ever did in my life was decide to write when I was in my mid-20s,” he said.

RELATED: ‘I Play Rocky’s Peter Farrelly Talks Why The Making Of ‘Rocky’ Was The Next Real-Life Story He Wanted To Take A Swing At – Toronto Studio

Farrelly also talked about his years and years of getting turned down for scripts and having to push through the rejection and not give up. As for 1994’s Dumb & Dumber, “We wrote that script five years before it got made, and it was the exact same script,” he shared. “Every studio passed, every actor passed.” He acknowledged that he changed the title to A Power Tool Is Not a Toy and “everyone started reading it.” It was the eventual film’s star Jim Carrey who told him Dumb & Dumber is the title, “so we went back to that,” he said.

“You’ve got to believe,, that they’re wrong and you’re right,” he said regarding the rejections. “You know, year after year getting turned down, it’s hard. You just gotta stick out and hope that you’re right.”

RELATED: ‘I Play Rocky’ Review: Peter Farrelly’s Sensational Biopic Of The Making Of Stallone’s Classic Is Just As Inspiring As The Original Movie – Toronto Film Festival

One of the most memorable and hilarious scenes in Farrelly’s films was in There’s Something About Mary and Cameron Diaz’s “hair gel.” “I realized we were going to have to go to R [in that film] because we were right on the PG-13 line.

“There’s two things that the studio didn’t want was the zipper joke in the beginning and the hair gel thing of course, and we understood that, especially the hair gel.” But of course, the hair gel stayed in and the rest is history.

Jesse Minter: "Very, very impressed" with how Jacoby Brissett picked up offense

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It appears quarterback Jacoby Brissett earned a good grade in his crash course on the Ravens offense.

Brissett joined the team a little over a week ago in a trade with the Cardinals and is on track to start against the Chargers on Monday night. In comments to reporters on Saturday, head coach Jesse Minter didn’t make it sound like Brissett will be limited to a small portion of the team’s playbook when he takes the field.

“The stuff you heard about was just like, ‘Hey, he’s a veteran presence and a real pro,’” Minter said, via the team’s website. “Obviously, he’s been around different systems and things like that. I would just say, I’m very, very impressed with how fast he’s learned what we’re doing.”

The Ravens head into Monday’s game in last place in the AFC North, so they’ll need Brissett to operate at a high level right out of the gate to stay in the thick of the playoff race heading into the back half of the season.

Chris Pratt “Would Love” to Make a Fourth ‘Guardians of the Galaxy’ Film With James Gunn “If He Wanted to” Amid DC Exit

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

"I love working with James [Gunn]. He’s the man. I haven’t had a chance to speak with him since that news came out," the actor said of Gunn's exit from DC Studios.

Chris Pratt “would love” to make a fourth Guardians of the Galaxy movie if James Gunn wanted to revisit the Marvel film franchise after his exit from DC Studios.

“That would be awesome,” Pratt told MovieZine when asked about the potential of a fourth film. “I love working with James [Gunn]. He’s the man. I haven’t had a chance to speak with him since that news came out… it’s interesting, because anytime something like this comes up and you say it, it kind of could become a headline.”

“So all I’ll say is I love James. He directed all three [Guardians] movies, and that’s pretty rare in those types of trilogies to have the same director through all three, so if he wanted to come back and bring it back, man, I would love that,” he added. Related Stories Movies James Gunn Says "No One From Skydance Has Seen" 'Man of Tomorrow,' Addresses "Untrue Rumors" Over 'Superman' Sequel Movies Skyler Gisondo Reacts to James Gunn, Peter Safran's DC Exits: "There's a Lot I Still Don't Know"

Pratt starred as Star-Lord/Peter Quill in all three Guardians of the Galaxy films for Marvel alongside Zoe Saldaña, Karen Gillan, Dave Bautista, Vin Diesel and Bradley Cooper. Pom Klementieff joined the cast in its second installment.

Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3 released in 2023, and was meant to be the final film in the Guardians franchise. Before the movie came out, it was revealed that Gunn and Peter Safran were set to become co-chairs and co-CEOs of DC Studios. It was announced last Monday that the duo will be replaced by Marvel alum Jeremy Latcham, who will now serve as president and chief creative officer of DC Studios.

Gunn and Safran were presumed safe from being removed from their posts at DC Studios amid the merge of Paramount and Warner Bros., though Skydance co-CEOs David Ellison and Ynon Kreiz opted to bring Latcham in.

Pirates Of The Caribbean 6 Return Addressed By Star After Johnny Depp Update

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After Johnny Depp's latest comments, another franchise star addresses returning for Pirates of the Caribbean 6.

During the press tour for his new movie, Ebenezer, Depp shared that he doesn't "know if you'll see us set sail. We might do that privately." He then revealed his Pirates of the Caribbean 6 return condition is that "if things are exactly where they need to be with regard to how they're going to top off the Pirates thing, the only involvement I really want to have truly is just to send them off on a better note."

In an interview with The Daily Beast, Pirates of the Caribbean star Mackenzie Crook, who plays Ragetti, was asked if he is open to returning after Depp's recent remarks. He replied, "Yes, I think I would for the hell of it. It would be so interesting – obviously it would depend on the script – to even just have a cameo to see what happened to those characters, Pintel and Ragetti, what happened to them after we last saw them. Again, I was really fond of those characters."

Crook also looked back on the making of the movies, admitting "at times it was grueling" while also emphasizing that "I was aware that this was sort of the end of an era. In one of the movies, a ship had to blow up, and so we got a ship and packed it with explosives and blew it up. And I don't think you'd do that now. It would all be CGI, but this sort of felt like the last remnants of old-fashioned movie making. It was special."

The bumbling, comedic, and fan-favorite pirate duo Ragetti and Pintel (Lee Arenberg) are in the first three Pirates of the Caribbean movies, in which they serve on the crews of both Captain Hector Barbossa (Geoffrey Rush) and Captain Jack Sparrow (Depp). They are last seen in Pirates of the Caribbean: At World's End with Barbossa aboard The Black Pearl after stealing the ship from Jack.

There were plans for Ragetti and Pintel to return in the fourth and fifth installments, On Stranger Tides and Dead Men Tell No Tales, but their appearances were cut from the former, and scheduling conflicts got in the way of coming back in the latter sequel. Now that Crook confirms he is open to reprising his role in Pirates of the Caribbean 6, Ragetti could appear for the first time since At World's End was released in 2007, both continuing his story and potentially providing closure for it.

Depp's return condition makes it clear that closure is what he is most interested in when it comes to the long-gestating movie. Producer Jerry Bruckheimer has spoken on numerous occasions about the project being in development and that there have been ongoing talks with Depp. Along with bringing back prior actors from the franchise, Margot Robbie has been reported as being attached to the film at various points, in what previously was being described as more of a reboot, while discussions in more recent years have been more indicative of a sequel featuring Jack.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

The movie still has a long way to go, and many crucial details need to be finalized, before getting greenlit. Depp's return to the public eye and mainstream movies with Ebenezer, and his comments about what it would take for him to play Jack again, has brought a renewed focus to making the sequel a reality, though, as have stars like Crook weighing in on also returning. Depp playing Jack again, possibly for the last time, would undoubtedly be the biggest draw, but bringing back fan-favorite characters like Ragetti would also tap into nostalgia for longtime fans, especially if he reunites with Pintel.

In the years between At World's End and the in-development Pirates of the Caribbean 6, Crook has played Looper in City of Ember, Orell in Game of Thrones, Andy Stone in The Detectorists, the voice of Hawkbit in Netflix's Watership Down miniseries, Gordon in Small Prophets, and the voice of Kreacher in Audible's full-cast audio editions of the Harry Potter books.

Amy's Original Doc Story Continuation Is Dead For Now

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Doc is finding fascinating new ways to evolve beyond its original premise. After a car accident left Molly Parker’s Dr. Amy Larsen unable to recall the last eight years of her life, she spent season 1 trying to remember who she was. Doc season 2 shifted from rediscovering Amy's identity to confronting the consequences of her past.

Initially, Amy couldn't remember the tragedies that led to her divorce from Dr. Michael Hamda (Omar Metwally), or that she had fallen in love with Dr. Jake Heller (Jon Ecker), the Chief Resident working directly under her. However, Amy's amnesia effectively reversed their professional and personal circumstances, stripping her of both her authority and her memories of their relationship.

Doc's messy love triangle rivals the romantic drama of Grey's Anatomy, with Michael remarried and expecting a child while pining for the new old Amy. Meanwhile, in the Doc season 2 finale, Amy realizes Jake still has feelings for his ex-wife. Recognizing that both men are holding on to the past, Amy decides to move forward on her own.

Doc season 3’s flashbacks have introduced another romantic entanglement: Blair Underwood’s Dr. Ben Grant, with whom Amy had an affair during those forgotten eight years. Entertainment journalist Matt Webb Mitovich stated in his newsletter, Matt's Inside Line, what Doc fans had already begun to suspect: Amy and Michael aren't likely to rekindle their romance anytime soon.

With Jake also moving on (or moving backward), the series appears ready to leave its original love triangle behind. However, Amy's forgotten past keeps creating new complications, ensuring Doc isn't running out of romantic drama anytime soon.

Despite their complicated history, Michael has remained one of Amy's strongest supporters, using his managerial position at Westside Hospital to help her rebuild her career. His second marriage even ended because of his lingering feelings for Amy, seemingly clearing the way for a romantic reconciliation. However, Doc season 3 has shown little interest in bringing the former spouses back together.

Instead, the series is doubling down on Amy's forgotten relationship with Dr. Grant, which was far more complicated than a simple affair. During their relationship, Amy consulted on Ben's medical research and agreed to overlook unfavorable data to strengthen his study. Now that the research is moving forward, Amy is gradually recovering her memories of the project.

Meanwhile, Michael may have a new romantic prospect too. As Mitovich noted in Matt's Inside Line, Sasha (Nazanin Boniadi), one of Ben's research partners, appears to have taken a "shine" to Michael. Although it's unclear whether their connection will develop into a full-fledged romance, Sasha's introduction further complicates the possibility of Michael reuniting with Amy.

More intriguingly, Sasha's professional connection to Ben places both Amy and Michael within the orbit of a potentially unethical medical research project. Rather than simply introducing another love interest to keep the former spouses apart, Doc could be setting up a storyline that intertwines their romantic histories with a larger professional scandal.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

After two seasons of Amy struggling to reconcile her past and present, the consequences of her forgotten choices could prove more explosive than the original love triangle. Nevertheless, there is only one person who Amy should be with at the series’ end.

Jake Heller would ultimately be the more satisfying romantic endgame for Doc's protagonist. Michael represents a life Amy can never fully reclaim, while Jake represents the possibility of moving forward. More importantly, Jake fell in love with Amy during one of the darkest periods of her life, accepting a version of her that Michael struggled to recognize.

Amy and Michael's marriage fell apart following the death of their son, as Amy's grief fundamentally changed her personality. Michael's romantic feelings were reawakened after her accident, when Amy reverted to the person she had been eight years earlier. However, loving someone means accepting every part of them, including the parts that are difficult to love.

Amy's colder, meaner personality is still part of her, even as she consciously works to become kinder and more compassionate. In contrast, Jake fell in love with Amy at her worst, finding something worth loving in a woman who had pushed nearly everyone else away.

Although their secret relationship lasted only a few months before her accident, finding love during such a dark period is arguably more meaningful than rekindling a romance from happier times. Their relationship also offers Amy the opportunity to build something new rather than recreate what she's lost.

Doc seasons 1 and 2 are streaming on Netflix, while all episodes are available on Hulu. New episodes air Tuesdays on Fox and stream on Hulu the following day.

Admittedly, Amy ended their relationship, and Jake is currently rekindling his romance with Rachel, his ex-wife and the mother of his daughter. However, Doc has hardly run out of compelling storytelling opportunities, and there's no telling where these characters might end up.

Perhaps Amy's true romantic partner hasn't even been introduced yet. But after watching her and Jake struggle to find their way back to each other over and over in Doc, an entirely new love interest would struggle to deliver the same emotional payoff.

A car accident leaves Dr. Amy Larsen with a brain injury, leaving her without the memories of the last eight years in this FOX medical drama. Relying on her estranged daughter and close friends to persevere through this new disability, Dr. Larsen starts over as an intern, rebuilding both her personal life and her medical practice career.

Prime Video's 40-Episode Flagship Franchise Is Secretly The Best Western On TV

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Faith Roswell is a Senior Writer on Screen Rant's Classic TV team. Since earning her degree in Creative Writing over a decade ago, Faith has written articles on film and TV from a variety of different angles. Faith now combines her knowledge of psychology with her love of monster movies to give more insight into what makes the best ones.  You may have read her Screen Rant lists and features covering horror, sci-fi, and fantasy, or read her Amazon Top 10 book, "Movie Monsters of the Deep." Faith has had an extensive career as a writer, appearing on BBC live radio, researching true crime for Rotten Mango podcast, and writing for publications including Mental Floss, Atlas Obscura, and The Daily Jaws before beginning here at Screen Rant.

A lone man walks into a small American town that is beset by problems and corruption. He makes an impression, has a fleeting romance with one of the local women, and when the trouble is over, he rides away into the sunset for an unknown destination. This storyline is instantly recognizable to classic Western fans, yet it has been cleverly hidden within one of Prime Video's best action TV shows that is not presented as a Western at all.

Alan Ritchson's performance as Jack "just Reacher" made it clear that Reacher would be a masterpiece, and while the show made some necessary changes from Lee Child's bestselling book series, it has been faithful to its format. The Jack Reacher novels are mostly standalone stories and maintaining this setup is one of the best decisions the creative team made, as it allowed new viewers to begin watching the series at any point. Still, Reacher and its spinoff, Neagley, are presented as action TV shows with the occasional horror, crime, and even neo-noir elements, but their true genre has gone unnoticed.

Reacher's latest season changed genres, with season 4 centering on a worldwide conspiracy and taking the franchise toward horror in its later episodes. However, this is an outlier, and Reacher's four seasons have mostly stayed closer to home, with small town mysteries and corruption that sees its titular character both coming up against law enforcement and working with sympathetic agents. This is a tried-and-tested formula for the Western genre, with Westerns featuring a mysterious drifter hero who mentors local police almost being a genre of their own, and ranging from The Magnificent Seven to Longmire.

Reacher's spinoff, Neagley, might have originally been met with skepticism, but Neagley has beaten Reacher's Rotten Tomatoes audience record by bringing something new to the table but keeping some of the formula Reacher introduced, and this includes its genre. Like Reacher, Frances Neagley has loner tendencies and a strict sense of justice, which both lead to her taking on a vigilante role when she encounters a wall of corruption. The themes of frontier justice are clear in the explosive first season, which takes her to a lawless rural commune with a cult leader.

Prime Video confirmed that Neagley has been renewed for a second season, and as the spinoff must break Reacher's rule and continue the story with a regular cast, this will have interesting implications. Rather than changing Neagley's genre, this looks set to take the show further along the Western genre path, but while Reacher remains "the man with no first name," Neagley can lead her own team as "the magnificent four."

After Reacher stopped a potentially world-changing series of events in the show's fourth season, the show is going to return to its roots with another small-town mystery. Reacher's fifth season is based on Lee Child's book Make Me, which is not just a claustrophobic and ominous story, but another change in genre for the series. The extremely disturbing subject matter will bring in both horror and psychological drama elements that canonically affect Reacher in the later books, which means that season 5 could be a turning point for both the show and the character.

Even so, with Reacher once again arriving in a remote area with hostile and watchful people, and forming a partnership with a woman with a vested interest in the town's secret, the show is also going back to its hidden genre: the Western. This extends to its setting, which takes him to the prairies and farming land of the rural midwest, which will act as the frontier town soon to face his brand of justice, before he inevitably heads off again. Whether Reacher will break from tradition and have company for the next season remains to be seen.

Without a phone and traveling light, former military police officer Jack Reacher drifts between American towns, embroiling himself in local crimes and bringing justice in his wake. Based on the series of novels by Lee Child, Reacher's journey offers him an intimate understanding of the nation he once served.

Netflix’s 10/10 ‘Slow Horses’ Replacement Officially Reclaims Streaming Crown

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

He has covered everything from Marvel to the Oscars, and Marvel at the Oscars. He also writes obsessively about the box office, charting the many hits and misses that are released weekly, and how their commercial performance shapes public perception. In his time at Collider, he has also helped drive diversity by writing stories about the multiple Indian film industries, with a goal of introducing audiences to a whole new world of cinema.

It's always tricky when a show takes longer than usual between seasons, especially in the streaming era. Audiences are accustomed to their favorite shows returning like clockwork every year, and that's one of the reasons why Prime Video's Reacher has been such a massive hit. The action series aired its fourth season this year and delivered record numbers. On the other hand, Netflix's The Gentlemen witnessed a 45% drop in opening-week viewership, presumably because of the two-year gap between seasons. Prime Video's The Terminal List, which spent four years off the air, failed to crack the standard 1 billion minutes watched milestone upon its return.

The jury's still out on Netflix's acclaimed spy thriller series, which premiered to 10.8 million views in 2024 and reclaimed the streamer's viewership crown upon its recent return. According to Netflix, the show clocked 3.8 million views in the first three days of release, but its actual opening-week performance will only become clear when the streamer shares its latest viewership report in the coming days. The show has the potential to become Netflix's answer to Slow Horses, which has emerged as a platform-defining hit for Apple TV over the past four years.

A key to Slow Horses' success is the strict production schedules that Apple TV has enforced. Two seasons are produced simultaneously, which is how the show has aired six seasons since 2022. Like Slow Horses, the Netflix series also consists of six episodes per season. However, it took two years for the show to return. The positive reviews that the show has received will only go so far in attracting audiences, but catching up on six episodes isn't a huge time commitment for new viewers.

We're talking, of course, about Black Doves. Starring Keira Knightley, Ben Whishaw, and Sarah Lancashire, the show reclaimed the number-one spot on Netflix worldwide upon its return on November 5. According to FlixPatrol, it's currently trailing the breakout hit The Body and the true-crime title Taken: Natalee Holloway.

Also starring Sam Riley, Ambika Mod, and Babou Ceesay, the new season of Black Doves has received critical acclaim. It's now sitting at a "Certified Fresh" 96% score on Rotten Tomatoes, up from Season 1's 92%. The aggregator website's consensus reads, "Black Doves takes flight once more in this fun-fueled second season where Keira Knightley and Ben Whishaw reign in the proceedings with verifiably pristine performances." Stay tuned to Collider for more updates.

Black Doves is a drama series that centers on the complex interplay of espionage and personal relationships. Focused on Helen Webb, a seasoned spy striving to balance her covert missions with a turbulent family life, the series delves into themes of loyalty, betrayal, and sacrifice. Starring Keira Knightley, it examines the moral ambiguities faced by those who live double lives in service to their countries.

Mads Mikkelsen Delivered His Best Performance In This Unforgettable 2012 Movie

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Few European actors have found the blend of native and international success that Mads Mikkelsen has. Born in Denmark, he became a star in his home country during the 1990s and 2000s, his career kicking off with Nicolas Winding Refn's 1996 crime thriller "Pusher." He's since become a favorite of Hollywood productions, too, starting with his role as the Bond villain Le Chiffre in "Casino Royale."

Since then, Mikkelsen has remained a go-to Hollywood bad guy, appearing in blockbusters like Marvel Studios' "Doctor Strange." He's also one of the privileged few to have played arguably the most famous screen villain ever, Hannibal Lecter. More than a few would argue Mikkelsen's Lecter in the 2013 TV series "Hannibal" is even better than Anthony Hopkins in "The Silence of the Lambs."

Mikkelsen's razor-sharp cheekbones, intense stare, and European accent instantly code him as "villain" in Hollywood language. When "Rogue One: A Star Wars Story" cast him as Galen Erso, a sympathetic scientist conscripted into building the Death Star, it read as casting against type to American audiences. But look at Mikkelsen's Danish resume and you'll find he's a much more diverse actor than the box that Hollywood has stuck him inside. Amusingly, one of those movies — Thomas Vinterberg's "The Hunt" — stars Mikkelsen as a good man mistakenly persecuted as a villain.

Lucas (Mikkelsen) is a Danish kindergarten teacher. One of his students, his best friend Theo's (Thomas Bo Larsen) daughter Klara (Annika Wedderkopp), has a puppy love crush on him. Obviously, he gently turns her down. Klara then vengefully suggests to the school principal that Lucas exposed himself to or molested her. (In reality, as the movie shows, her crude older brother shoved an iPad displaying porn in her face.) The ugly rumor grows out of control, and Lucas' reputation pays the price.

In "The Hunt," the town rushes to the worst possible conclusion about Lucas. All but a few loved ones like his son, Marcus (Lasse Fogelstrøm), believe the worst from hearsay. But they don't actually know it for a fact (indeed, what ultimately helps clear Lucas is the facts pointing in his favor). The movie is never ambiguous, though; we know from the very beginning that Lucas is innocent and this is all just a terrible tragedy of miscommunication. "The Hunt" also avoids reactionary narratives about scorned women peddling false rape allegations because Klara is a young child. She simply doesn't grasp the consequences of what she did, as is the case with the other preschoolers who echo her claims.

Compare a similar recent film, "Fjord," inspired by a real-life story about a Norwegian evangelical couple whose children were taken from them after suspicions of abuse. Like in "The Hunt," the allegations are implied to stem from leading questions and miscommunication from the kids. But "Fjord" counterintuitively stacks the deck in the parents' favor while trying to present objectivity, perhaps because it knows many left-leaning audiences are inclined to side against the conservative couple and takes perverse enjoyment in testing those sympathies.

A film like "The Hunt" could choose not to reveal Lucas' innocence either way or cast doubt, inviting you to sympathize with a possible monster as an endurance test. But the reality of "The Hunt," seeing an innocent man be branded with a scarlet letter, is also agonizing to watch in its own way. It's a modern iteration on the parable of the feathers in the wind, and how once spoken, even untruthful gossip can't be erased.

As Lucas, Mikkelsen sheds any of the power or menace he projects as a villain in those aforementioned Hollywood movies. "The Hunt" is also a Danish-language film, and Mikkelsen's comfort with his native tongue also makes for an even more seamless performance.

To this day, I've never forgotten the catharsis of a scene where Lucas stands up for himself. After being assaulted in a grocery store, he walks in again, headbutts the hostile clerk, and demands his groceries back. The first time I watched "The Hunt," I rewound the movie to see the scene a second time.

That relatively late-in-the-movie scene is a well-earned respite from Lucas' victimhood. Even in a story like this, too much passivity from a protagonist turns into a chore. Lucas similarly takes matters into his own hands at the movie's emotional climax. Again, Klara's father Theo is Lucas' best friend; while Theo takes Klara's side, he claims to know Lucas so well he can tell when he's lying or truthful by just looking into his eyes. Lucas goes to Christmas Mass, putting himself dead center in the community that has exiled him, and looks at Theo across the pews.

The power of this lynchpin scene is in Mikkelsen's silent, icy stare (Lucas pointedly ditches his glasses during this scene). But it's not the aloof stare of Le Chiffre or the predatory one of Hannibal Lecter. In his sad eyes and slight frown, Lucas is begging Theo to read the truth on his face. When that's not enough, he storms over to Theo, sobbing, and refuses to let him look away from his eyes.

Mads Mikkelsen is an extraordinary actor, and yet the greatest proof of that is him playing a normal man in "The Hunt."

A baby turkey with a feather duster ‘mom’ has a new BFF

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 14, 2026 10:03 AM EST

It seems straight out of a Disney movie—wild animals from two different species becoming inseparable friends. And yet that’s what happened at the Raven Ridge Wildlife Center in southeastern Pennsylvania, between a turkey and a mourning dove.

Last spring, the wildlife center received two likely orphaned turkey poults (young turkeys). To calm them down, the team put them with a feather-duster as a stand-in mother turkey. Because turkey poults are easily stressed, they are difficult to rehabilitate, according to a Facebook post by the wildlife center. In fact, one of them didn’t make it.

“Since we never raise baby animals alone, we had to find a companion for the remaining poult,” the wildlife center wrote. “We paired it with a baby mourning dove of similar size, hoping they would comfort each other while eating and exploring as they are both ground eaters.”

The pairing turned out to be a raging success. They formed an inseparable bond that Raven Ridge Wildlife Center had never seen before. Months later, the team gave the dove a soft release, meaning the bird could leave whenever it wanted. But he clearly had no intention of leaving his friend. The dove continued returning to the turkey’s cage, and the two would sit near each other on either side of the separation.

“After a few days, we put the dove back in with the turkey, and it was clear they were happiest together,” the wildlife center explained. But “we knew keeping them caged for the rest of their lives, or keeping them just because they were cute, was neither an option nor fair to these wild animals.”

As such, the team contacted a large sanctuary in Pennsylvania to request a joint release. This way, the duo could choose whether to stick together or follow their own paths. After their soft release, the dove flew into the trees and the turkey started foraging, free to stick together or join other members of their species.

“We don’t know if they’ll stay together, but our team made sure they had a safe place until they were ready to be on their own,” the wildlife center concluded.

Liam Neeson's New Action Thriller Officially Removed From Almost 1,000 Theaters After Just 1 Weekend

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ever since Taken transformed Liam Neeson into an unlikely action superstar, the Oscar-nominated actor has spent nearly two decades proving that age is no obstacle when it comes to beating up bad guys. From Non-Stop and The Commuter to The Ice Road, Neeson has become one of Hollywood's most dependable names for audiences looking for a straightforward thriller. Unfortunately, that reputation hasn't always translated into box office success, particularly as mid-budget action movies have increasingly found their audiences on streaming, and his latest might need that to happen again.

Neeson's new action thriller The Mongoose was officially dropped from 826 theaters after just one weekend, falling from 1,014 locations to only 188 following a disastrous $464,421 domestic opening. The Samuel Goldwyn Films release debuted on October 30, 2026, averaging just $458 per theater during its first three days.

The situation didn't improve during its second weekend, when The Mongoose earned only $79,842, representing an 83% decline. Its domestic total currently stands at approximately $777,000, making it a particularly disappointing result for a movie that initially received a release in more than 1,000 theaters.

The collapse is especially striking given Neeson's history of carrying action thrillers to respectable theatrical returns. However, the film's modest opening suggested that audiences simply weren't turning out for his latest adventure, leaving exhibitors with little reason to retain it.

When war hero Ryan “Fang” Flanagan (Neeson) accidentally kills his ex-wife’s abusive new fiancé in self- defense, he knows how it will look. Accused of a crime he didn’t commit and with nothing to lose, Fang steals a Shelby Mustang and makes a desperate bid for freedom. As he leads the police in a cross-country car chase, he is aided by his former Special Forces Army comrades. When the chase sparks the interest of local news channels, Fang ends up closely monitored on national TV with a sympathetic public rooting for his safe getaway.

The film stars Liam Neeson (Taken) as Ryan "Fang" Flanagan, Marisa Tomei (Spider-Man: No Way Home) as Tara, Ving Rhames (Mission: Impossible) as Tanker, Michael Chiklis (The Shield) as Pope, and Marcus Thomas (The Forger) as Munch. The movie is directed by Mark Vanselow.

The Mongoose opened in US theaters on October 30, 2026. A streaming premiere date has not yet been announced. Stay tuned to Collider for all the latest news and updates on the biggest and best movies and shows heading to your favorite streaming platforms soon.

Netflix's 8-Part Female John Wick Series Is One Of Its Best Binges

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

It’s rare for any thriller to live up to the John Wick franchise, let alone a small-screen show, but one Netflix series with a single season managed this feat. It is tough to think of an action thriller movie from the last 15 years that was more influential than 2014’s John Wick. In terms of pure plot, the Keanu Reeves vehicle was the simple story of a retired assassin killing every criminal involved in his dog’s death.

However, in visual and aesthetic terms, John Wick spelled the death knell for the shaky cam style that dominated action thrillers in the 2000s and early 2010s thanks to the popularity of director Paul Greengrass’s groundbreaking Bourne sequels. After John Wick’s release, the movie’s sequels popularized a hyper-smooth, slick action filmmaking style that prioritized gliding, unbroken Steadicam shots over fast-cutting.

Soon enough, Atomic Blonde, Bullet Train, Extraction, the Nobody movies, Kate, Gunpowder Milkshake, Pretty Lethal, Violent Night, and almost every other action thriller movie had embraced this CGI-assisted filmmaking style. Few of these efforts could match the John Wick sequels, and even John Wick’s own streaming spinoff The Continental ended up feeling like a knock off rather than a legitimate franchise entry. This is why it is so impressive that Netflix’s under-seen South Korean thriller series My Name recaptured John Wick’s entire appeal perfectly.

As an action thriller about revenge, My Name sounds straightforwardly similar to John Wick in terms of plot. However, the complicated lore of the show’s story makes My Name closer in story terms to the Internal Affairs movies or their Americanized re-imagining, The Departed. Han So-hee’s Yoon Ji-woo is the daughter of a powerful mob member who joins the city's police so she can act as a mole and pass on information to the Dongcheon drug cartel.

Yoon Ji-woo’s boss, the kingpin Choi Mu-jin, encourages her to spy on the police, meaning the show’s heroine soon lands a spot on the very task force that is investigating the cartel. However, once there, she learns from her new detective partner that Choi Mu-jin is suspected to have killed her father. With split loyalties, Yoon Ji-woo has no one to trust and every reason to sharpen her combat skills. While My Name’s plot is as complex as any crime drama like Love/Hate, this show is no slow burn, realistic character study.

Instead, Netflix’s hyper-stylized My Name is a neon-drenched thrill ride, as Yoon Ji-woo battles her way through the city’s criminal underworld while trying to solve the mystery of her father’s sudden death. Gritty, dark, and fast-paced, the action show is a must-watch for fans of revenge thrillers.

Every action show needs to find the right balance between constant fights, chases, and gun battles, but My Name’s tense premise gives the story an incredible head start in this regard. My Name’s twisty plot and the complicated machinations of its heroine’s plan mean that even the show’s quiet dialogue scenes crackle with constant tension.

There is always a risk that Yoon Ji-woo will be exposed as a mole in either organization, which ramps up tension during quiet scenes and makes the action all the more hard hitting. Even compared to dark crime thrillers like This City Is Ours, My Name’s one-season story is a masterclass in tension building.

On that note, the show’s one-season status actually works for, rather than against, My Name as a series on the whole. While it would have been hard to drag My Name’s story out for more than one season, Netflix’s killer John Wick replacement wisely limited its story to just one perfectly paced outing instead.

RHOP’s Karen Huger Shares Update on Ray Huger Marriage After Season 11

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Real Housewives of Potomac's Karen Huger exclusively told E! News at the Hunting Wives season two premiere where she stands with husband Ray Huger as they plan the holidays with daughter Rayvin.

Karen Huger has no illusions of grandeur about the future of her marriage.

While the Real House of Potomac's Grand Dame admitted viewers would have to stay tuned to see how she and husband Ray Huger continue to work on their relationship during season 11, she confessed issues with her partner of 32 years haven't gone away.

"We're going through a rough patch," Karen exclusively told E! News at the Hunting Wives season two premiere Nov. 12, crediting her ability to face their struggles to the fact that "Ray and I have always been honest."

And despite the difficulties in her relationship since her return home after serving six months in prison due to a 2024 DUI conviction, the reality star has a positive outlook about their family unit with daughter Rayvin Huger.

As Karen explained, "The one thing is, we will always be a family, and we will always be friends."

And because of the couple's unbreakable bond, their plans for the holidays remain a family affair where the motto is the more the merrier.

"We're going to celebrate as a family," Karen shared. "In fact, we are going down to Virginia, near the guest house that I just renovated, to celebrate with our entire family."

As for how the 63-year-old feels about her equally complicated relationship with costar Wendy Osefo?

"I'm hoping for the best for Wendy. I'm always in her corner," Karen remarked. "We are supporting her 100 percent and wishing for the best."

Aside from her Bravo friend group, Karen is also a big fan of the women of Maple Brook, Texas, with the second season of Hunting Wives arriving on Netflix Nov. 26. In fact, she effused about her favorite actress in the drama, Brittany Snow.

As Karen put it, "Brittany Snow? Love, love, love. Sign me up. I'm ready to kill someone, OK."

With that in mind, Karen is open to trying out her acting chops alongside the Hunting Wives cast, musing when it comes to a future cameo, "All things are possible."

3 Best Movies To Watch On Prime Video This Weekend (November 14-15)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From that lineup, only the latter is currently trending on the streaming service nationwide, as the post-apocalyptic thriller Apocalypse Z: New World currently stands as the 9th most popular movie on Prime Video's Top 10 Movies chart in the United States. As such, it remains a good option as we head into the weekend, just like the first film in the franchise, Apocalypse Z: The Beginning of the End.

Last weekend's recommendations were a comedy, a psychological thriller, and a post-apocalyptic thriller. This time around, I have shifted the list towards sci-fi, with one of the films being a masterpiece cyberpunk release that stands as one of the most iconic sci-fi movies to have ever been released. Joining it is an action thriller with a sci-fi twist to it.

The only non-sci-fi movie on our new Prime Video list is a spy comedy, which promises to be the film for the whole family on the streaming service this weekend. These are the best movies to watch on Prime Video this weekend.

Starting the list is the most different release, Agent Cody Banks, the spy comedy starring Frankie Muniz and Hilary Duff, which is like a teenaged James Bond adventure. Given how the casting of Daniel Craig's replacement as James Bond is the talk of the town in Hollywood, with actors like Robert Pattinson and Nicholas Hoult joining the race, I felt that Agent Cody Banks was a timely choice.

That said, do not expect the film to go as dark and gritty as many of the 007 movies, given how Agent Cody Banks relies heavily on Muniz's comedic timing from his experience in the sitcom Malcolm in the Middle. This one is worth watching for the leads' chemistry, the spy gadgets, the fast-paced action, and the light take on material like James Bond.

Infinite starts to lean our list towards sci-fi, with the Mark Wahlberg release currently standing as the most popular movie on Prime Video in the United States. Infinite is the perfect option for those who are looking for action this weekend, but want something grittier and a lot more mature than Agent Cody Banks.

Infinite follows Wahlberg as Evan McCauley, a man who deals with mysterious visions, has abilities that he does not remember training to develop, and more. However, when he learns from a group, the Infinites, that these visions are memories of past lives, everything changes, and he must face a dangerous foe. Infinite is not groundbreaking, but the film is worth watching for its intense action and talented ensemble, which also includes Dylan O'Brien, Chiwetel Ejiofor, Jason Mantzoukas, and more.

Finally, Blade Runner: The Final Cut was one of the early October arrivals on Prime Video, and anyone who follows the masterpiece sci-fi cyberpunk franchise started by director Ridley Scott must know why it is a timely movie. After all, Prime Video's Blade Runner 2099 series will be released on the streaming service on November 25, so now is the time to start the franchise or rewatch it, if you have not already.

This first Blade Runner movie set up an immersive and intricate world where the line between human and machine is blurred. Harrison Ford delivers a magnetic performance in the role of Rick Deckard, and the film helped set the tone for live-action cyberpunk movies and TV shows. All those factors make the morally complex thriller a must-watch on Prime Video this weekend.

3 Elite Prime Video Series to Binge This Weekend (Nov 14-15)

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

2026 has been a strong year for Prime Video, but thoughts are now turning to 2027 as the cold nights set in and Christmas approaches. Recently, two of the streamer's most popular shows announced their 2027 release dates. Off Campus Season 2 will debut on April 28, 2027, and Benito Skinner's acclaimed comedy Overcompensating will return on February 17, 2027. But before thoughts turn entirely to 2027, there's still plenty more to be enjoyed before the year ends. With that in mind, here's a look at three must-watch shows on Prime Video this weekend.

For more recommendations, check out our list of the best shows and movies on Prime Video.

Is it possible to recommend anything this weekend without mentioning The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power? The smash-hit fantasy series began with an acclaimed outing in 2022, before stumbling with a divisive second installment. Earlier this week, the show returned for a third installment, now five years after the fall of Eregion and with a terrifying war between the Elves and Sauron (Charlie Vickers) in full swing.

"The Rings of Power Season 3 successfully balancing what fans know will happen alongside the journey that will take them there is what makes for good TV," wrote Collider's Therese Lacson in her review of Season 3, one of several that were full of praise. Morfydd Clark, Robert Aramayo, Owain Arthur, Sophia Nomvete, Ismael Cruz Córdova, and more all star in the third season, which continues with two episodes on November 18.

As mentioned previously, it was recently announced that the hit 2026 series Off Campus ​​​​​​will return in April next year. With that in mind, whether you're new to the show or could benefit from a rewatch, why not binge all eight episodes this weekend? Based on the novels by Elle Kennedy and adapted for the screen by Louisa Levy, the series follows music student Hannah (Ella Bright) and star hockey player Garrett (Belmont Cameli) as a "fake relationship" blossoms into reality.

For fans of romance shows like Bridgerton and Heated Rivalry, this is a must-watch indulgence. Called "exactly what romance novel lovers want from a TV adaptation" in Collider's review of the series, Off Campus is bursting with heart and drama, with each episode sure to keep you hooked. Bright and Cameli are perfectly cast as Hannah and Garrett, although the main romantic focus of Season 2 shifts away from the pair.

If you've been enjoying Ben Watkins' new series The Greatest, all about the early life of Muhammad Ali, on Prime Video, then you simply can't miss his most famous production, Cross. Aldis Hodge stars as the unorthodox detective Alex Cross from the James Patterson novels, as his complicated and fascinating personal and professional lives combine as tricky crimes are solved.

Don't expect a light mystery series with Cross, as this gritty, psychological thriller will demand your full attention. The series stars Isaiah Mustafa and Juanita Jennings alongside Hodge, and has released 16 episodes so far across two seasons. Amazon MGM Studios renewed the series for an eight-episode third season, which is expected in spring next year, so make sure you catch up in time.

James Gunn Says “No One From Skydance Has Seen” ‘Man Of Tomorrow’: “There Are A Lot Of Untrue Rumors Going Around”

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

James Gunn is clearing up some rumors following his exit as co-head of DC Studios.

With Skydance making changes amid the Paramount and Warner Bros. merger, Peter Safran and Gunn announced they would not be heading DC Studios.

Amid their exits, many rumors have circulated. One prominent story was that Skydance saw the Superman sequel, titled Man of Tomorrow, and planned to make some edits, but Gunn has shut down those allegations.

“There are a lot of untrue rumors going around, but one of them is that Skydance has seen Man of Tomorrow and that all sorts of sweeping changes are being made,” Gunn posted on Threads.

He continued, “No one from Skydance has even seen the movie – the only people that have are Peter Safran and a very small trusted group of friends and family. So any stories around this narrative are false.” Watch on Deadline

Man of Tomorrow wrapped filming in August, with Gunn sharing a joyful post and celebrating everyone involved in the project.

“That’s a wrap on principal photography for Man of Tomorrow,” Gunn shared on Threads. “Truly a joyful experience, and I have immense gratitude to my cast and crew.”

RELATED: ‘Constantine’ Director Feels “More Hopeful” For Sequel Following James Gunn & Peter Safran’s DC Studios Exit

Man of Tomorrow is set for release on July 9, 2027, with David Corenswet reprising his role as Clark Kent. The cast also includes Nicholas Hoult as Lex Luthor, Lars Eidinger as Brainiac, Rachel Brosnahan as Lois Lane, Skyler Gisondo as Jimmy Olsen, Sara Sampaio as Eve Teschmacher, Isabela Merced as Hawkgirl, Nathan Fillion as Guy Gardner, Edi Gathegi as Mister Terrific, and Adria Arjona as Maxima.

Aaron Pierre, who plays John Stewart in HBO’s Lanterns, is set to jump to the big screen in the Superman sequel. Also set to reprise his Blue Beetle role is Xolo Maridueña, who will play the superhero once again since the 2023 film, directed by Ángel Manuel Soto.

Amid the executive shakeup at Skydance, former Marvel exec Jeremy Latcham is now set to run DC Studios.
